    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results are convincing, demonstrating significant performance gains over established MLLMs on a diverse set of benchmarks. The ablation studies effectively isolate the contributions of individual components.
    *   **Resource Efficiency:**  The achievement of SOTA performance with fewer vision tokens makes FUSION a more practical and scalable solution.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Releasing code, model weights, and the dataset promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Well-Reasoned Design:** The design choices are clearly motivated and well-supported by theoretical arguments and empirical evidence.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Synthetic Data:** The heavy reliance on synthetic data for training, while innovative, raises concerns about potential biases and limitations in real-world scenarios. More evaluation and experiments with real-world scenarios are needed.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper does not extensively discuss the computational cost associated with text-guided vision encoding and recursive alignment decoding. A thorough analysis of computational complexity would be valuable.
    *   **Generalizability:** While benchmarks are strong, further evaluation across different domains and applications would strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   **Dialogue Challenges:** The paper acknowledges that Text-Guided Unified Vision Encoding has limitations on its handling of multi-turn dialogue in the Appendix, and this could be viewed as a major limitation of the proposed approach. The paper would have been stronger if it had addressed these limitations and came up with a solution.

*   **Potential Influence:** FUSION has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It could inspire the development of more deeply integrated vision-language architectures.
    *   It could encourage the use of text-guided vision encoding to improve visual understanding.
    *   It could promote the creation of more targeted and high-quality synthetic datasets for MLLM training.
    *   It can serve as a benchmark for future developments.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to vision-language integration, resulting in significant performance improvements and resource efficiency. The comprehensive integration strategy, strong empirical results, and the release of resources make it a valuable contribution to the field. While the heavy reliance on synthetic data and potential computational overheads are valid concerns, they are outweighed by the paper's strengths and its potential influence on future research. In addition, the paper has provided well-motivated ablation studies that clearly highlight the importance of each of the proposed components, and a starting point to future researchers. In all, it makes a valuable contribution to the field, and thus deserves a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Efficient Task-specific Conditional Diffusion Policies: Shortcut Model Acceleration and SO(3) Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09927v1)
- **Authors**: Haiyong Yu, Yanqiong Jin, Yonghao He, Wei Sui
- **Abstract**: Imitation learning, particularly Diffusion Policies based methods, has recently gained significant traction in embodied AI as a powerful approach to action policy generation. These models efficiently generate action policies by learning to predict noise. However, conventional Diffusion Policy methods rely on iterative denoising, leading to inefficient inference and slow response times, which hinder real-time robot control. To address these limitations, we propose a Classifier-Free Shortcut Diffusion Policy (CF-SDP) that integrates classifier-free guidance with shortcut-based acceleration, enabling efficient task-specific action generation while significantly improving inference speed. Furthermore, we extend diffusion modeling to the SO(3) manifold in shortcut model, defining the forward and reverse processes in its tangent space with an isotropic Gaussian distribution. This ensures stable and accurate rotational estimation, enhancing the effectiveness of diffusion-based control. Our approach achieves nearly 5x acceleration in diffusion inference compared to DDIM-based Diffusion Policy while maintaining task performance. Evaluations both on the RoboTwin simulation platform and real-world scenarios across various tasks demonstrate the superiority of our method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Classifier-Free Shortcut Diffusion Policy (CF-SDP) for efficient task-specific action generation in embodied AI. It addresses the limitations of traditional Diffusion Policy methods, which suffer from slow inference speeds due to iterative denoising. The key contributions are: (1) integrating classifier-free guidance with shortcut-based acceleration for faster inference while maintaining performance and (2) extending diffusion modeling to the SO(3) manifold for stable and accurate rotational estimation. Experimental results in both simulation and real-world scenarios demonstrate significant speed improvements (nearly 5x) compared to DDIM-based Diffusion Policy, along with competitive task performance. The method effectively leverages task-specific conditions to guide action generation and uses a novel approach for handling rotations within the SO(3) group.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates a strong combination of existing techniques (classifier-free guidance, shortcut models, SO(3) diffusion) to tackle a practical problem in embodied AI. The core novelty lies in the synergistic integration of these components and their application within the context of robotic control. This addresses a specific pain point: slow inference of diffusion policies hindering real-time robotic applications. The extension of diffusion to the SO(3) manifold is not entirely new, but the specific implementation within a shortcut model and the accompanying analysis of its benefits for robotic control contribute to the novelty.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the practicality of diffusion policies for real-world robotics. The 5x speedup in inference is a substantial improvement. Furthermore, by formulating the problem within the SO(3) manifold, the authors address the challenges of representing and controlling rotations accurately, which is essential for robotic manipulation tasks. The empirical results, while obtained on a set of specific tasks, provide compelling evidence of the method's effectiveness. The real-world experiments help to validate the simulator results.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing diffusion policies and the challenges of real-time robotic control.
*   **Sound Technical Approach:** The proposed CF-SDP combines established techniques in a novel and well-justified manner. The integration of classifier-free guidance, shortcut models, and SO(3) diffusion is logically presented.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental evaluation is thorough, including comparisons to a baseline (DDIM-based Diffusion Policy) and ablation studies. The results demonstrate the effectiveness of CF-SDP in both simulation and real-world scenarios.
*   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is generally well-written and organized, making it easy to follow the technical details and understand the key contributions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Task Diversity:** While the experiments cover various tasks, they are still somewhat limited in scope. The performance may vary significantly across different robotic platforms and task complexities. The real-world experiments are confined to just a few tasks of the same category.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** It mentions that the Transformer-based architecture was very sensitive to hyperparameters. While it focused on the CNN-based one as a result, it leaves one wondering whether further hyperparameter optimisation could allow this approach to match or even exceed the CNN based approach in terms of performance.
*   **Generalization Concerns:** The reliance on task-specific conditions raises concerns about the generalizability of the method to new tasks or environments. While classifier-free guidance aims to mitigate this, it's not clear how well CF-SDP would perform in truly unseen scenarios.
*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration of techniques is strong, each individual component has been previously explored. This slightly diminishes the overall level of novelty.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper is likely to influence research in robotic control, particularly in the application of diffusion policies. The demonstrated efficiency improvements and the SO(3) representation could inspire further work on developing more practical and robust diffusion-based methods for embodied AI. The integration of classifier-free guidance with shortcut models could become a common approach for accelerating diffusion-based policies.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a technically sound and empirically validated approach for improving the efficiency and accuracy of diffusion policies for robotic control. While the individual components are not entirely novel, their combination and application to this specific problem are significant. The 5x speedup in inference and the improved rotational estimation are valuable contributions.

The primary weaknesses are the somewhat limited task diversity in the evaluation and the potential concerns regarding the generalizability of the method. More experiments with a wider range of robots, tasks and environment would make the results stronger. Due to these limitations, while the paper demonstrates a significant practical improvement, its theoretical impact is moderate. Therefore, it is assigned a score of 7, reflecting a solid contribution to the field with the potential for further impact.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Constrained Auto-Regressive Decoding Constrains Generative Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09935v1)
- **Authors**: Shiguang Wu, Zhaochun Ren, Xin Xin, Jiyuan Yang, Mengqi Zhang, Zhumin Chen, Maarten de Rijke, Pengjie Ren
- **Abstract**: Generative retrieval seeks to replace traditional search index data structures with a single large-scale neural network, offering the potential for improved efficiency and seamless integration with generative large language models. As an end-to-end paradigm, generative retrieval adopts a learned differentiable search index to conduct retrieval by directly generating document identifiers through corpus-specific constrained decoding. The generalization capabilities of generative retrieval on out-of-distribution corpora have gathered significant attention. In this paper, we examine the inherent limitations of constrained auto-regressive generation from two essential perspectives: constraints and beam search. We begin with the Bayes-optimal setting where the generative retrieval model exactly captures the underlying relevance distribution of all possible documents. Then we apply the model to specific corpora by simply adding corpus-specific constraints. Our main findings are two-fold: (i) For the effect of constraints, we derive a lower bound of the error, in terms of the KL divergence between the ground-truth and the model-predicted step-wise marginal distributions. (ii) For the beam search algorithm used during generation, we reveal that the usage of marginal distributions may not be an ideal approach. This paper aims to improve our theoretical understanding of the generalization capabilities of the auto-regressive decoding retrieval paradigm, laying a foundation for its limitations and inspiring future advancements toward more robust and generalizable generative retrieval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the generalization limitations of constrained auto-regressive decoding in generative retrieval (GR).  It examines the problem from two perspectives: the constraints imposed by specific corpora and the use of beam search during document identifier (docID) generation. The authors derive a theoretical lower bound on the error introduced by corpus-specific constraints, showing its dependence on the Simpson diversity index of the relevance distribution. They also demonstrate that beam search, due to its reliance on marginal distributions, can lead to poor recall despite good precision, especially for sparse relevance distributions.  The theoretical findings are validated through experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets. The core message is that the inherent limitations of constrained auto-regressive generation need to be understood and addressed for GR to achieve robust generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its theoretical analysis of *why* generative retrieval might struggle to generalize, especially when constrained to specific corpora.  While previous work has acknowledged the generalization problem, this paper attempts to provide a more fundamental understanding by analyzing the interaction between constrained decoding, beam search, and the underlying relevance distributions.  The derivation of the lower bound on the KL divergence due to constraints and the analysis of beam search limitations are arguably novel contributions. The decomposition of the negative effect into constraints and beam search is a notable aspect of the study.
*   **Significance:** Understanding the theoretical limitations of GR is crucial for its further development and wider adoption.  By identifying the impact of constraints and beam search, the paper provides valuable insights that can guide future research in designing more robust and generalizable GR models. The implications of the concentration of relevance distributions as a key factor are particularly important. This opens avenues for future work on improving the recall of GR models.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical Rigor:** The paper presents a solid theoretical analysis, providing mathematical justifications for its claims. The use of KL divergence and Simpson diversity index provides a formal framework for understanding the limitations.
    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines the problem of generalization in GR and isolates the impact of constrained decoding.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The theoretical findings are supported by experiments on both synthetic and real-world datasets.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper suggests potential avenues for improvement, such as balancing concentration during model training.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplifying Assumptions:** The theoretical analysis relies on certain simplifying assumptions, such as equal document lengths and i.i.d. sampling of the downstream corpus, which may not fully reflect real-world scenarios. The analysis of beam search might benefit from exploring more sophisticated beam search variants.
    *   **Limited Real-World Evaluation:** While the real-world experiments provide some support for the theoretical findings, the scale and complexity of the evaluation could be increased to further validate the results. The evaluation on real-world datasets also highlights the challenges in replicating the theoretical conditions.
    *   **Limited Generalizability of results:** The results are sensitive to the parameters and assumptions.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's theoretical insights can influence future research directions in generative retrieval. It encourages researchers to consider the impact of constraints and beam search when designing GR models and to develop techniques for mitigating these limitations. It may also inspire new decoding strategies that are less reliant on marginal distributions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a valuable theoretical contribution to the field of generative retrieval by analyzing the limitations of constrained auto-regressive decoding. The work is novel and the use of a solid mathematical justification adds depth and credibility. However, the theoretical assumptions and somewhat limited real-world validation keep it from reaching the highest levels of impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### KeepKV: Eliminating Output Perturbation in KV Cache Compression for Efficient LLMs Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09936v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Tian, Zihan Wang, Yebo Peng, Aomufei Yuan, Zhiming Wang, Bairen Yi, Xin Liu, Yong Cui, Tong Yang
- **Abstract**: Efficient inference of large language models (LLMs) is hindered by an ever-growing key-value (KV) cache, making KV cache compression a critical research direction. Traditional methods selectively evict less important KV cache entries based on attention scores or position heuristics, which leads to information loss and hallucinations. Recently, merging-based strategies have been explored to retain more information by merging KV pairs that would be discarded; however, these existing approaches inevitably introduce inconsistencies in attention distributions before and after merging, causing output perturbation and degraded generation quality. To overcome this challenge, we propose KeepKV, a novel adaptive KV cache merging method designed to eliminate output perturbation while preserving performance under strict memory constraints. KeepKV introduces the Electoral Votes mechanism that records merging history and adaptively adjusts attention scores. Moreover, it further leverages a novel Zero Inference-Perturbation Merging methods, keeping attention consistency and compensating for attention loss resulting from cache merging. KeepKV successfully retains essential context information within a significantly compressed cache. Extensive experiments on various benchmarks and LLM architectures demonstrate that KeepKV substantially reduces memory usage, enhances inference throughput by more than 2x and keeps superior generation quality even with 10% KV cache budgets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "KeepKV: Eliminating Output Perturbation in KV Cache Compression for Efficient LLMs Inference":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of KV cache compression in large language model (LLM) inference. Existing methods for KV cache compression often lead to information loss, hallucinations, and output perturbations. KeepKV proposes a novel adaptive KV cache merging method to eliminate output perturbation while preserving performance under memory constraints. Key components of KeepKV include: (1) the Electoral Votes mechanism, which records merging history and adaptively adjusts attention scores; and (2) Zero Inference-Perturbation Merging (ZIP-Merging), which maintains attention consistency and compensates for attention loss resulting from cache merging.  The paper provides theoretical analysis and experimental results showing that KeepKV reduces memory usage, enhances throughput, and maintains generation quality, even with limited KV cache budgets. The core idea is to not just compress but also to maintain the information integrity during the compression process by intelligently adjusting weights.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to KV cache compression. The Electoral Votes mechanism and ZIP-Merging are innovative concepts that address a key limitation of existing merging-based methods: output perturbation. The idea of preserving historical information during merging is a significant step forward. The theoretical grounding of the method, by attempting to minimize output perturbation, is also a notable contribution compared to prior work that relies more on heuristics. However, similarity-based candidate selection and attention-based adaptation have been explored before, so KeepKV's novelty primarily lies in the unique combination and theoretical justification of these techniques.

*   **Significance:** The KV cache is a major bottleneck in LLM inference, especially for long contexts.  The paper tackles this issue effectively, presenting a method that improves performance and reduces memory usage while maintaining generation quality. This has significant practical implications for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. If the method is widely adopted, it could contribute to wider accessibility of LLMs and enable faster inference times. The thorough experimental evaluation on various benchmarks and LLM architectures strengthens the paper's significance. The theoretical guarantees for the perturbation bound are also a significant contribution that lends weight to the empirical findings. However, its benefits for very large context lengths and high compression rates remain to be thoroughly explored.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong theoretical foundation with provable guarantees on output perturbation.
    *   Novel approach that directly addresses the problem of attention inconsistency in KV cache merging.
    *   Extensive experimental validation on various benchmarks and LLM architectures.
    *   Clear and well-written paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Complexity: Implementing the Electoral Votes mechanism and ZIP-Merging might add some complexity to the inference pipeline, though the paper argues that is outweighed by its efficiency advantages.
    *   Parameter tuning: The paper mentions setting the merging threshold and exponential prediction coefficient. The sensitivity of the performance to these parameters and the optimal way to tune them could be explored further.
    *   Incremental Benefit: While the paper demonstrates better performance than other baselines, the incremental improvement over state-of-the-art methods might be small in certain conditions.
    *   Longer context validation: Most results are up to 8K, but the real benefit of these memory savings is enabling longer context lengths, so showing results on context lengths of 16k+ would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of KV cache compression research. The idea of minimizing output perturbation and preserving historical information could inspire new approaches to merging-based methods. The theoretical analysis provides a solid foundation for future research in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** KeepKV presents a novel and theoretically grounded approach to KV cache compression that effectively addresses the issue of output perturbation.  The experimental results are convincing, and the potential for practical impact is significant. While there are some weaknesses related to complexity and parameter tuning, the strengths of the paper outweigh these concerns. The theoretical analysis is strong, and the novelty is apparent as it makes progress beyond similarity-based candidate selection. The paper introduces a framework for preserving the information integrity during compression, moving beyond purely focusing on memory savings. It lacks comprehensive testing at high context lengths, which caps the score. However, its innovation and potential significance justify a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Omni-Dish: Photorealistic and Faithful Image Generation and Editing for Arbitrary Chinese Dishes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09948v1)
- **Authors**: Huijie Liu, Bingcan Wang, Jie Hu, Xiaoming Wei, Guoliang Kang
- **Abstract**: Dish images play a crucial role in the digital era, with the demand for culturally distinctive dish images continuously increasing due to the digitization of the food industry and e-commerce. In general cases, existing text-to-image generation models excel in producing high-quality images; however, they struggle to capture diverse characteristics and faithful details of specific domains, particularly Chinese dishes. To address this limitation, we propose Omni-Dish, the first text-to-image generation model specifically tailored for Chinese dishes. We develop a comprehensive dish curation pipeline, building the largest dish dataset to date. Additionally, we introduce a recaption strategy and employ a coarse-to-fine training scheme to help the model better learn fine-grained culinary nuances. During inference, we enhance the user's textual input using a pre-constructed high-quality caption library and a large language model, enabling more photorealistic and faithful image generation. Furthermore, to extend our model's capability for dish editing tasks, we propose Concept-Enhanced P2P. Based on this approach, we build a dish editing dataset and train a specialized editing model. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of our methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Omni-Dish: Photorealistic and Faithful Image Generation and Editing for Arbitrary Chinese Dishes":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Omni-Dish, a novel text-to-image generation model specifically designed for Chinese dishes. Recognizing the limitations of existing general-purpose models in capturing the nuances and details of culturally specific cuisine, the authors propose a comprehensive approach involving a large-scale dish dataset, a recaption strategy, and a coarse-to-fine training scheme. Furthermore, they extend the model's capabilities to dish editing tasks by proposing Concept-Enhanced P2P, a method for constructing a dish editing dataset and training a specialized editing model. Through extensive experiments and evaluations, the authors demonstrate the superiority of Omni-Dish in generating photorealistic and faithful images of Chinese dishes, as well as its effectiveness in dish editing tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in addressing a specific gap in the text-to-image generation field: the generation and editing of culturally specific dishes, particularly Chinese cuisine. While text-to-image generation and image editing are well-established areas, the specialization and focus on a specific domain, complete with a dedicated pipeline for data curation and targeted training strategies, adds a valuable contribution. The introduction of Concept-Enhanced P2P to improve consistency in dish editing is also a novel contribution.
* **Significance:** The paper holds significant potential for impact on the food industry and e-commerce, as it addresses the increasing demand for high-quality, culturally distinctive dish images. The detailed approach to data curation, training, and evaluation also presents a valuable resource for researchers interested in developing specialized text-to-image generation models for other domains. The demonstrated ability to generate realistic and detailed images of Chinese dishes, and to perform specific edits, can greatly benefit online food ordering platforms, recipe websites, and other food-related applications.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Data Pipeline:** The paper presents a robust data curation pipeline, including filtering, correction, and tagging, which is crucial for building a high-quality dish dataset. The use of LLMs and VLLMs within this pipeline is well-justified and effectively utilized.
    *   **Recaption Strategy:** The two-stage recaption strategy using both LLMs and VLLMs addresses a key challenge of acquiring detailed and accurate descriptions of dish images, leading to better image generation results.
    *   **Concept-Enhanced P2P:** The proposed Concept-Enhanced P2P approach for dish editing is a novel and effective way to improve the consistency and quality of edited dish images.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation, including both automated metrics and human evaluations, across multiple dimensions, which strengthens the credibility of the results.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** While the specialization is a strength, it is also a limitation. The model's architecture and training strategies are specifically tailored to Chinese dishes, and it may not be directly generalizable to other cuisines or domains without significant modifications.
    *   **Black Box Model:** The adoption of FLUX, Qwen2.5-7B leaves the process as somewhat of a black box with limited analysis of the inner workings of the generated images.
    *   **Dataset size:** Given the 100 million image-text pairs collected, it is unknown how many images remain after the filtering pipeline. This begs the question of if it would be better to have better data collection.

**Justification of Score:**

Despite the limited generalizability, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of text-to-image generation and image editing by focusing on a specific and relevant domain: Chinese cuisine. The detailed approach to data curation, training, and evaluation, along with the novel Concept-Enhanced P2P method, demonstrates the potential of specialized models for capturing the nuances and details of specific domains. Therefore, a score of 7 is justified.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### C-MTCSD: A Chinese Multi-Turn Conversational Stance Detection Dataset
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09958v1)
- **Authors**: Fuqiang Niu, Yi Yang, Xianghua Fu, Genan Dai, Bowen Zhang
- **Abstract**: Stance detection has become an essential tool for analyzing public discussions on social media. Current methods face significant challenges, particularly in Chinese language processing and multi-turn conversational analysis. To address these limitations, we introduce C-MTCSD, the largest Chinese multi-turn conversational stance detection dataset, comprising 24,264 carefully annotated instances from Sina Weibo, which is 4.2 times larger than the only prior Chinese conversational stance detection dataset. Our comprehensive evaluation using both traditional approaches and large language models reveals the complexity of C-MTCSD: even state-of-the-art models achieve only 64.07% F1 score in the challenging zero-shot setting, while performance consistently degrades with increasing conversation depth. Traditional models particularly struggle with implicit stance detection, achieving below 50% F1 score. This work establishes a challenging new benchmark for Chinese stance detection research, highlighting significant opportunities for future improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces C-MTCSD, a large Chinese multi-turn conversational stance detection dataset built from Sina Weibo. The dataset contains 24,264 annotated instances, making it significantly larger than the existing CANT-CSD dataset. The authors comprehensively evaluate the dataset using both traditional deep learning models and large language models (LLMs). Their results highlight the challenges of C-MTCSD, showing that even state-of-the-art models struggle, especially in the zero-shot setting and with increasing conversation depth. The paper establishes C-MTCSD as a new benchmark for Chinese stance detection research, demonstrating significant opportunities for future work.  The authors also note issues related to implicit stance expression and target reference in conversational contexts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary contribution is the dataset itself, C-MTCSD. The novelty lies in its size and the focus on Chinese multi-turn conversational data, addressing a gap in the availability of resources for this specific task. The analysis of dataset characteristics (depth, stance distribution) is also valuable. However, while the experiments showcase the challenges of the dataset, the baselines used are relatively standard, and the methodological novelty is limited.

*   **Significance:** The dataset has the potential to significantly impact the field of Chinese stance detection. The lack of large, high-quality datasets has been a bottleneck, and C-MTCSD addresses this directly. By providing a challenging benchmark, it can drive the development of more sophisticated models that can handle conversational context and implicit stance. The zero-shot results highlight the difficulty and potential impact of models that can transfer knowledge across topics. However, the paper's significance depends on the community's adoption of the dataset and whether it truly spurs innovation in model development. The success of the dataset will be measured by future research directly leveraging and improving on current results.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Significant increase in the size of Chinese conversational stance detection datasets.
    *   Comprehensive analysis of the dataset's characteristics.
    *   Benchmark experiments using a range of models, including LLMs.
    *   Clear demonstration of the challenges of conversational stance detection.
    *   Focus on a less explored language.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited methodological novelty. The experiments primarily use existing models.
    *   The models tested although modern, may be outdated given the speed of LLM advancement.
    *   The introduction section frames previous techniques as if they have not advanced past individual sentences. While they were designed for the original task as such, most techniques can be modified to process sentence-by-sentence, and then a sequence learner could process the stance detections as a sequence.
    *   The paper does not explore the use of other techniques such as dialogue-state trackers, which may be relevant to the task.

*   **Impact:** The paper's immediate impact is limited by the relatively small number of citations since it was published. However, the potential long-term impact could be substantial if researchers in the community adopt the dataset and begin developing new models specifically tailored for the challenges it presents.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The creation of the C-MTCSD dataset is a valuable contribution, addressing a clear need in the field of Chinese conversational stance detection. The dataset's size and complexity represent a significant step forward. The comprehensive evaluation provides a useful benchmark for future research. However, the limited methodological novelty and the reliance on existing models prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. The score reflects the balance between the dataset's potential impact and the incremental nature of the experimental contributions.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Privacy Meets Explainability: Managing Confidential Data and Transparency Policies in LLM-Empowered Science
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09961v1)
- **Authors**: Yashothara Shanmugarasa, Shidong Pan, Ming Ding, Dehai Zhao, Thierry Rakotoarivelo
- **Abstract**: As Large Language Models (LLMs) become integral to scientific workflows, concerns over the confidentiality and ethical handling of confidential data have emerged. This paper explores data exposure risks through LLM-powered scientific tools, which can inadvertently leak confidential information, including intellectual property and proprietary data, from scientists' perspectives. We propose "DataShield", a framework designed to detect confidential data leaks, summarize privacy policies, and visualize data flow, ensuring alignment with organizational policies and procedures. Our approach aims to inform scientists about data handling practices, enabling them to make informed decisions and protect sensitive information. Ongoing user studies with scientists are underway to evaluate the framework's usability, trustworthiness, and effectiveness in tackling real-world privacy challenges.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Privacy Meets Explainability: Managing Confidential Data and Transparency Policies in LLM-Empowered Science."

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the growing concern of confidential data leakage when using Large Language Models (LLMs) in scientific workflows, particularly in the context of Intellectual Property (IP). The authors propose a framework called "DataShield" to mitigate these risks. DataShield comprises three main modules: a confidential data detection module, a policy summarization module, and a visualization dashboard. The confidential data detection module identifies sensitive terms like gene and protein names within user prompts. The policy summarization module extracts and summarizes privacy policies of external tools used by LLMs, aligning them with organizational codes of conduct, generating easy-to-understand "privacy nutrient labels." The visualization dashboard offers a comprehensive view of data flow, actions triggered by the LLM, and interactions with third-party tools, showing detected confidential data, relevant privacy policies, and compliance assessments. The paper also outlines user studies planned to evaluate the framework's usability, trustworthiness, and effectiveness among research scientists, including details about local, open-source LLMs and synthetic data to protect data integrity during the user study. Preliminary results show the performance of the confidential data detection module versus other NER tools.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper demonstrates novelty by tackling a relatively underexplored aspect of privacy in LLMs: the leakage of confidential *organizational* data (IP, proprietary information), going beyond the typical focus on Personally Identifiable Information (PII).  The integration of three distinct modules—detection, summarization, and visualization—into a single framework is a significant contribution. Policy summarization through LLMs and the generation of privacy nutrition labels are not brand new concepts, but the integration of knowledge graph based methods (PoliGraph) *and* aligning external tool policies with *internal* company policies does add novelty.  The focus on LLM agents and their integration with external tools is also timely and relevant, highlighting a real-world privacy challenge. The human-in-the-loop aspects, while not conceptually groundbreaking, contribute to making the framework more usable and responsive to the needs of scientific users. The use of localised LLMs and synthetic data for user studies is also a valuable feature from a privacy perspective.

*Significance:*

The significance of the paper lies in its potential to bridge the gap between the power of LLMs for scientific discovery and the necessity for safeguarding sensitive data.  The framework's potential to empower scientists to make informed decisions about data handling is significant. The preliminary results of the confidential data detection module also showcase the feasibility of this approach, although there's clearly room for improvement based on the performance metrics compared to other tools (precision especially lagging).

*Strengths:*

*   Addresses a real and pressing problem in the scientific use of LLMs.
*   The multi-module framework provides a holistic solution.
*   The focus on usability and user understanding is commendable.
*   The user study is well-designed with a focus on maintaining data integrity and user safety.
*   Discussion of limitations and future directions.
* Well-structured and clearly written.

*Weaknesses:*

*   The paper primarily describes the design and architecture of the framework; the empirical evaluation is limited to preliminary results on the data detection module.  The effectiveness of the policy summarization and visualization modules remains to be demonstrated.
*   The reliance on LLMs for policy summarization and contextual detection introduces the risk of hallucinations and biases.  While the paper mentions the RAG approach and local LLMs, the potential for these issues needs to be addressed more thoroughly.
*   The confidential data detection module, as presented, currently focuses on gene and protein data. The framework should be easily extensible and adaptable to other scientific data types (materials, chemicals, etc.).
* While the paper mentions indirect data exposure, it does not provide concrete solutions to addressing it.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper could influence the development of more responsible and privacy-aware LLM-based tools for scientific research.  It could also inform organizational policies and best practices for data handling in the age of AI. The emphasis on explainability and transparency aligns with broader trends in responsible AI development.  Further, it could encourage more research into the specific challenges of confidential data management in scientific LLM workflows.

*Justification for Score:*

While the paper offers a novel and significant approach to managing confidential data in LLM-powered scientific workflows, the limited empirical evaluation and dependence on LLMs for key components are weaknesses. The current implementation is also only applicable to a certain domain, restricting its scope. It also seems like DataShield is built on top of many other technologies, so its singular contribution is potentially diminished. Therefore, the score is based on the potential influence and sound design rather than demonstrated impact.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enhancing Multi-task Learning Capability of Medical Generalist Foundation Model via Image-centric Multi-annotation Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09967v1)
- **Authors**: Xun Zhu, Fanbin Mo, Zheng Zhang, Jiaxi Wang, Yiming Shi, Ming Wu, Chuang Zhang, Miao Li, Ji Wu
- **Abstract**: The emergence of medical generalist foundation models has revolutionized conventional task-specific model development paradigms, aiming to better handle multiple tasks through joint training on large-scale medical datasets. However, recent advances prioritize simple data scaling or architectural component enhancement, while neglecting to re-examine multi-task learning from a data-centric perspective. Critically, simply aggregating existing data resources leads to decentralized image-task alignment, which fails to cultivate comprehensive image understanding or align with clinical needs for multi-dimensional image interpretation. In this paper, we introduce the image-centric multi-annotation X-ray dataset (IMAX), the first attempt to enhance the multi-task learning capabilities of medical multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) from the data construction level. To be specific, IMAX is featured from the following attributes: 1) High-quality data curation. A comprehensive collection of more than 354K entries applicable to seven different medical tasks. 2) Image-centric dense annotation. Each X-ray image is associated with an average of 4.10 tasks and 7.46 training entries, ensuring multi-task representation richness per image. Compared to the general decentralized multi-annotation X-ray dataset (DMAX), IMAX consistently demonstrates significant multi-task average performance gains ranging from 3.20% to 21.05% across seven open-source state-of-the-art medical MLLMs. Moreover, we investigate differences in statistical patterns exhibited by IMAX and DMAX training processes, exploring potential correlations between optimization dynamics and multi-task performance. Finally, leveraging the core concept of IMAX data construction, we propose an optimized DMAX-based training strategy to alleviate the dilemma of obtaining high-quality IMAX data in practical scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of effectively training medical generalist foundation models for multi-task learning.  It argues that simply scaling data or enhancing architectural components is insufficient.  Instead, it emphasizes a data-centric approach, highlighting the limitations of decentralized multi-annotation X-ray datasets (DMAX) where individual images aren't consistently used across multiple tasks. The paper introduces IMAX, a new image-centric multi-annotation X-ray dataset. IMAX is built around the principle that each X-ray image should be densely annotated with multiple labels and textual descriptions spanning a wide range of medical tasks (calculation, report generation, classification, REC, REG, VQA). They compare fine-tuning several open-source medical MLLMs with IMAX versus DMAX, demonstrating significant multi-task performance improvements with IMAX.  The paper also investigates the optimization dynamics using the Fisher Information Matrix (FIM) and proposes a DMAX-based training strategy to approximate IMAX when high-quality dense annotation is challenging to obtain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The most significant contribution is the IMAX dataset itself. While multi-task learning in medical imaging is not entirely new, the emphasis on *image-centric* dense annotation as a key to unlocking better performance from foundation models is a valuable insight. Prior work has often focused on aggregating existing datasets, leading to fragmented task representation for each image. The analysis of FIM during training also provides some novel insights into how the data influences optimization. The optimized DMAX training strategy is a useful practical addition, though not deeply novel.

*   **Significance:**  The paper demonstrates a clear performance improvement with IMAX, substantiating its claim about the importance of data organization. The results are convincing, with experiments across multiple models.  This is important because it suggests a shift in focus from simply scaling data to thoughtfully structuring it for effective multi-task learning.  It provides a blueprint for future dataset construction efforts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem statement and motivation.
    *   Creation of a valuable new dataset (IMAX).
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple models.
    *   Insightful analysis of optimization dynamics using FIM.
    *   Practical DMAX-based training strategy for resource-constrained scenarios.
    *   Clear and well-structured writing.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The analysis using FIM, while interesting, could be more deeply explored. There are limitations to drawing causal inferences from correlations.
    *   The "high-quality" aspect of IMAX is somewhat subjective. While the authors claim high-quality curation, this isn't rigorously quantified beyond mentioning sources and careful data cleaning. Independent validation of the annotation quality would be beneficial.
    *   The "optimized" DMAX training strategy relies on pseudo-labeling which inherently introduces biases. A deeper discussion about the limitations and potential biases introduced by this approach is needed.
    * The experiments focus solely on X-rays. It is unclear if the image-centric approach will generalize well to other modalities.
    * The paper primarily relies on existing models. A more ambitious approach could involve building a generalist model trained solely on IMAX.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research directions in medical imaging and multi-task learning. It encourages a more deliberate and thoughtful approach to dataset construction. Other researchers are likely to build upon the IMAX dataset. The paper can be easily adapted to other tasks.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The image-centric dataset is more realistic and can have an impact in the field for model development.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**  The IMAX dataset and the performance gains demonstrated are valuable contributions, warranting a higher score.  However, the limitations in the FIM analysis, the need for more rigorous annotation validation, and the reliance on existing models prevent it from achieving a truly exceptional score. Nonetheless, the paper offers a practical and insightful contribution that can significantly advance the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Semi-implicit-explicit Runge-Kutta method for nonlinear differential equations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09969v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyun Ding
- **Abstract**: A semi-implicit-explicit (semi-IMEX) Runge-Kutta (RK) method is proposed for the numerical integration of ordinary differential equations (ODEs) of the form $\mathbf{u}' = \mathbf{f}(t,\mathbf{u}) + G(t,\mathbf{u}) \mathbf{u}$, where $\mathbf{f}$ is a non-stiff term and $G\mathbf{u}$ represents the stiff terms. Such systems frequently arise from spatial discretizations of time-dependent nonlinear partial differential equations (PDEs). For instance, $G$ could involve higher-order derivative terms with nonlinear coefficients. Traditional IMEX-RK methods, which treat $\mathbf{f}$ explicitly and $G\mathbf{u}$ implicitly, require solving nonlinear systems at each time step when $G$ depends on $\mathbf{u}$, leading to increased computational cost and complexity. In contrast, the proposed semi-IMEX scheme treats $G$ explicitly while keeping $\mathbf{u}$ implicit, reducing the problem to solving only linear systems. This approach eliminates the need to compute Jacobians while preserving the stability advantages of implicit methods. A family of semi-IMEX RK schemes with varying orders of accuracy is introduced. Numerical simulations for various nonlinear equations, including nonlinear diffusion models, the Navier-Stokes equations, and the Cahn-Hilliard equation, confirm the expected convergence rates and demonstrate that the proposed method allows for larger time step sizes without triggering stability issues.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a semi-implicit-explicit (semi-IMEX) Runge-Kutta (RK) method designed for solving nonlinear differential equations of the form  `u' = f(t, u) + G(t, u)u`, where *f* represents a non-stiff term and *Gu* a stiff term.  The key idea is to treat *G(t,u)* explicitly while keeping *u* implicit, which allows for solving only linear systems at each time step, reducing computational cost compared to traditional IMEX-RK methods that require solving nonlinear systems.  The paper presents a family of semi-IMEX RK schemes with varying orders of accuracy and tests them on various nonlinear equations like nonlinear diffusion models, Navier-Stokes equations, and the Cahn-Hilliard equation. Numerical results show the expected convergence rates and demonstrate the ability to use larger time steps without instability issues.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of treating the *G(t,u)* term explicitly while keeping the solution *u* implicit does offer a practical advantage in terms of computational efficiency. Standard IMEX schemes often struggle with the nonlinearity of *G*, forcing the solution of nonlinear systems. The implicit treatment of just *u* results in a linear system, a significant simplification. However, the overall concept of combining explicit and implicit treatments is not entirely new; IMEX methods are well-established. The novelty lies in *how* the splitting is performed, targeting a specific structure in the differential equation.
* **Significance:** The potential impact of this method stems from its ability to handle stiff nonlinear PDEs more efficiently. Many real-world problems, like those arising in fluid dynamics and materials science, are modeled by such equations. If the method proves to be robust and widely applicable, it could become a valuable tool for researchers in these fields. However, the paper's significance hinges on the method's scalability to higher-dimensional problems and its adaptability to different types of nonlinearities.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The main strength is the reduction in computational cost by solving only linear systems.
    *   **Stability:** The numerical results suggest that the method maintains good stability properties, allowing for larger time steps.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and the numerical experiments are clearly presented.
* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper shows numerical convergence, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the method's stability properties (e.g., A-stability, B-stability for nonlinear problems) would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Specific Form of the Equation:** The method is tailored to equations of the form *u' = f(t, u) + G(t, u)u*.  While this structure is common, its applicability to more general nonlinear equations may be limited without significant modification.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to Other Methods:** The comparisons primarily focus on IMEX with linear splitting. Comparing against other state-of-the-art approaches for stiff nonlinear ODEs (e.g., Rosenbrock methods with inexact Newton, exponential integrators) would provide a better understanding of its relative advantages.
    *   **Code Availability:** It is commendable that the authors provided code to reproduce the results.
*   **Scalability Considerations:** The computational benefits of the new method compared to other existing approaches are not clear for very high-dimensional and complicated models. A thorough complexity analysis is missing.

*   **Potential Influence:** The method has the potential to influence the field of numerical methods for PDEs, particularly for problems where the stiff term can be naturally expressed in the form *G(t, u)u*. However, its impact will depend on its adaptability to different types of problems and its performance compared to existing methods.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6**. While the paper presents a worthwhile contribution by offering a computationally efficient semi-IMEX RK method, it lacks the depth and breadth of analysis necessary for a higher score.  The idea of explicit treatment of G(t,u) is potentially valuable, but the limited theoretical analysis and comparison to other existing methods, and the high dependence of the method's performance on the specific structure of the differential equation reduce its overall significance. If the method can be extended to more complex scenarios and its robustness proven theoretically, the score would be higher.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: math.NA
- **Score**: 6/10

### OctGPT: Octree-based Multiscale Autoregressive Models for 3D Shape Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09975v1)
- **Authors**: Si-Tong Wei, Rui-Huan Wang, Chuan-Zhi Zhou, Baoquan Chen, Peng-Shuai Wang
- **Abstract**: Autoregressive models have achieved remarkable success across various domains, yet their performance in 3D shape generation lags significantly behind that of diffusion models. In this paper, we introduce OctGPT, a novel multiscale autoregressive model for 3D shape generation that dramatically improves the efficiency and performance of prior 3D autoregressive approaches, while rivaling or surpassing state-of-the-art diffusion models. Our method employs a serialized octree representation to efficiently capture the hierarchical and spatial structures of 3D shapes. Coarse geometry is encoded via octree structures, while fine-grained details are represented by binary tokens generated using a vector quantized variational autoencoder (VQVAE), transforming 3D shapes into compact \emph{multiscale binary sequences} suitable for autoregressive prediction. To address the computational challenges of handling long sequences, we incorporate octree-based transformers enhanced with 3D rotary positional encodings, scale-specific embeddings, and token-parallel generation schemes. These innovations reduce training time by 13 folds and generation time by 69 folds, enabling the efficient training of high-resolution 3D shapes, e.g.,$1024^3$, on just four NVIDIA 4090 GPUs only within days. OctGPT showcases exceptional versatility across various tasks, including text-, sketch-, and image-conditioned generation, as well as scene-level synthesis involving multiple objects. Extensive experiments demonstrate that OctGPT accelerates convergence and improves generation quality over prior autoregressive methods, offering a new paradigm for high-quality, scalable 3D content creation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OctGPT, a novel multiscale autoregressive model for 3D shape generation. OctGPT leverages a serialized octree representation to efficiently capture the hierarchical and spatial structures of 3D shapes. Coarse geometry is encoded via octree structures, while fine-grained details are represented by binary tokens generated by a vector quantized variational autoencoder (VQVAE). The model incorporates octree-based transformers with 3D rotary positional encodings, scale-specific embeddings, and a token-parallel generation scheme to address computational challenges associated with long sequences.  Experiments demonstrate OctGPT's versatility across various tasks, including text-, sketch-, and image-conditioned generation, as well as scene-level synthesis, showcasing improved efficiency and generation quality compared to prior autoregressive methods and rivaling diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper demonstrates several innovative aspects. The combination of octree representations with autoregressive models isn't entirely new (e.g., building upon the Octree Transformer work), but the specific approach of serializing octrees using binary splitting signals *and* VQVAE-encoded fine-grained details, coupled with the architectural improvements to the transformer, represents a significant advancement. Extending rotary positional encodings to 3D space (RoPE3D) and using scale-specific embeddings are also valuable contributions. The token parallelization is borrowing from prior work, but its adaptation to the octree structure is novel.  The core novelty lies in the clever representation that makes the autoregressive approach competitive and tractable.

* **Significance:**  The main contribution is demonstrating that autoregressive models *can* be highly effective for 3D shape generation, closing the gap with diffusion models. This is significant because autoregressive models offer potential advantages for multimodal integration (easier to combine with text or image models) and control.  The efficiency gains are also crucial; making high-resolution 3D generation feasible on relatively modest hardware is important for accessibility. The ability to handle scene-level synthesis is also a valuable extension.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong results:** OctGPT achieves state-of-the-art or near state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks, demonstrating its effectiveness. The qualitative results are compelling.
    * **Efficient architecture:** The architectural modifications (octree-based attention, RoPE3D, etc.) are well-motivated and lead to substantial improvements in training and generation speed. The ablations provide evidence for the effectiveness of these components.
    * **Versatility:**  The model performs well across different generation tasks (unconditional, conditional, scene synthesis), highlighting its flexibility.
    * **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand. The method is clearly explained, and the experiments are well-designed.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on VQVAE:** The two-stage pipeline (VQVAE + transformer) introduces a potential bottleneck. An end-to-end trainable model might yield even better results, although it would likely be more complex to train.
    * **Limited analysis of diversity:** While the paper mentions diversity, a more in-depth analysis using metrics beyond FID would be beneficial. The Chamfer distance plot provides *some* insight, but more robust measures of shape diversity could strengthen the evaluation.
    * **Incremental improvement on Octree Transformer**: The paper is positioned as an improvement over previous autoregressive methods. While the experimental results are strong, the paper doesn't provide sufficient detail regarding the relative benefits and tradeoffs compared to the Octree Transformer.

* **Potential Influence:** OctGPT is likely to influence future research in 3D shape generation, particularly in autoregressive modeling.  It provides a blueprint for designing efficient and effective autoregressive models for 3D data.  The approach of serializing octrees with VQVAE-encoded details could be adopted or adapted in other 3D generation tasks. The methods can potentially find applications in fields like game development, virtual reality, and computer-aided design where efficient generation of 3D assets is crucial.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of 7.  While OctGPT doesn't completely revolutionize the field, it represents a significant and well-executed advancement.  It tackles a challenging problem (efficient 3D shape generation with autoregressive models) with a clever representation and architectural innovations.  The experimental results are strong, and the paper is clearly written. The reliance on a VQVAE and limited diversity analysis are the main drawbacks, preventing it from scoring higher. It demonstrates the viability of autoregressive models by efficiently capturing both the overall structure using octrees and fine details through VQVAEs, using an architecture that builds upon prior work and delivers demonstrably better performance. Given these combined factors, a score of 7 seems appropriate.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Do We Really Need Curated Malicious Data for Safety Alignment in Multi-modal Large Language Models?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10000v1)
- **Authors**: Yanbo Wang, Jiyang Guan, Jian Liang, Ran He
- **Abstract**: Multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) have made significant progress, yet their safety alignment remains limited. Typically, current open-source MLLMs rely on the alignment inherited from their language module to avoid harmful generations. However, the lack of safety measures specifically designed for multi-modal inputs creates an alignment gap, leaving MLLMs vulnerable to vision-domain attacks such as typographic manipulation. Current methods utilize a carefully designed safety dataset to enhance model defense capability, while the specific knowledge or patterns acquired from the high-quality dataset remain unclear. Through comparison experiments, we find that the alignment gap primarily arises from data distribution biases, while image content, response quality, or the contrastive behavior of the dataset makes little contribution to boosting multi-modal safety. To further investigate this and identify the key factors in improving MLLM safety, we propose finetuning MLLMs on a small set of benign instruct-following data with responses replaced by simple, clear rejection sentences. Experiments show that, without the need for labor-intensive collection of high-quality malicious data, model safety can still be significantly improved, as long as a specific fraction of rejection data exists in the finetuning set, indicating the security alignment is not lost but rather obscured during multi-modal pretraining or instruction finetuning. Simply correcting the underlying data bias could narrow the safety gap in the vision domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the factors contributing to safety alignment in multi-modal large language models (MLLMs). It challenges the conventional wisdom that high-quality, curated malicious data is essential for enhancing MLLM safety. The authors argue that the alignment gap between text-only and multi-modal prompts primarily stems from training data distribution biases rather than image content or the quality of rejection responses. They demonstrate that fine-tuning MLLMs on a small set of benign instruction-following data with simple rejection sentences can significantly improve model safety, suggesting that the security alignment is present but obscured during pretraining and instruction fine-tuning. Essentially, the paper proposes a method to correct data bias with benign data instead of requiring extensive curated harmful data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Challenging Assumptions:** The paper directly challenges the common belief that curated malicious data is a necessity for safety alignment in MLLMs. This is a valuable contribution as it encourages a re-evaluation of current approaches.
    *   **Clear Experimental Design:** The authors conduct a series of ablation studies to isolate the key factors influencing MLLM safety. This systematic approach strengthens their claims and makes the paper more convincing.
    *   **Practical Approach:** The proposed method of fine-tuning on benign data with rejection responses offers a practical and less resource-intensive alternative to collecting and curating malicious data.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** They perform qualitative analysis and explore the impact of the method on downstream VQA tasks and rejection rates. They also present in-depth analysis of their results in different configurations and scenarios.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses mainly on mitigating typographic attacks and keyword incitement. The generalizability of the proposed method to other types of multi-modal attacks (e.g., adversarial image examples) remains unclear.
    *   **Over-Rejection Concerns:** While the paper addresses the potential for over-rejection, the impact on the model's overall utility and helpfulness requires further investigation.
    *   **Dependency on Existing LLM Alignment:** The method relies on the pre-existing safety alignment in the language module. It does not address how to create a robust safety alignment from scratch.
    *   **Evaluation could be extended:** The paper mentions "MM-SafetyBench" and "FigStep" as the main benchmarks. While important, it is unclear how these approaches cover the full range of "safety alignment". It would add value to have some additional metrics or comparisons.
    *   **Not all "safety" concerns are necessarily equivalent:** In the evaluation and throughout, the different facets of "safety alignment" are not fully distinguished. For example, health consultation and privacy violations are different.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The primary novelty lies in the counter-intuitive finding that data distribution bias is more important than curated adversarial data for this kind of safety. The approach leverages the knowledge within LLMs, thus saving engineering resources on curating data. This could significantly simplify the process of aligning MLLMs.
    *   The potential influence on the field is high, if the results generalize to other types of multimodal jailbreaks. The findings could lead to more efficient and scalable safety alignment techniques.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed study that challenges existing assumptions about MLLM safety alignment. The findings are potentially significant, offering a practical alternative to current methods. However, the limitations regarding scope, over-rejection concerns, and dependency on existing LLM alignment prevent it from achieving a higher score. The paper is clearly written and easy to follow.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### NaviDiffusor: Cost-Guided Diffusion Model for Visual Navigation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10003v1)
- **Authors**: Yiming Zeng, Hao Ren, Shuhang Wang, Junlong Huang, Hui Cheng
- **Abstract**: Visual navigation, a fundamental challenge in mobile robotics, demands versatile policies to handle diverse environments. Classical methods leverage geometric solutions to minimize specific costs, offering adaptability to new scenarios but are prone to system errors due to their multi-modular design and reliance on hand-crafted rules. Learning-based methods, while achieving high planning success rates, face difficulties in generalizing to unseen environments beyond the training data and often require extensive training. To address these limitations, we propose a hybrid approach that combines the strengths of learning-based methods and classical approaches for RGB-only visual navigation. Our method first trains a conditional diffusion model on diverse path-RGB observation pairs. During inference, it integrates the gradients of differentiable scene-specific and task-level costs, guiding the diffusion model to generate valid paths that meet the constraints. This approach alleviates the need for retraining, offering a plug-and-play solution. Extensive experiments in both indoor and outdoor settings, across simulated and real-world scenarios, demonstrate zero-shot transfer capability of our approach, achieving higher success rates and fewer collisions compared to baseline methods. Code will be released at https://github.com/SYSU-RoboticsLab/NaviD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "NaviDiffusor: Cost-Guided Diffusion Model for Visual Navigation."

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a hybrid approach to visual navigation that combines learning-based methods (diffusion models) with classical cost-based planning. It trains a conditional diffusion model on path-RGB observation pairs to learn a prior over valid paths.  During inference, it guides the diffusion model's path generation by incorporating the gradients of differentiable, scene-specific (collision avoidance) and task-level (goal seeking) costs. This allows the system to leverage the strengths of both approaches: learning-based methods for implicit scene understanding and classical methods for explicit constraint satisfaction. The method demonstrates zero-shot transfer capabilities and improved success rates and collision avoidance compared to baselines in simulated and real-world environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the specific *integration* of cost guidance with a diffusion model for visual navigation.  While both diffusion models and cost-based planning are established techniques, the way this paper combines them is novel.  Specifically, injecting the cost gradient *during* the denoising process of the diffusion model is a key contribution, as opposed to using the diffusion model simply as a black box path generator. This approach allows the learned prior to be constrained by external guidance to meet task-specific demands, something lacking in purely learning-based methods. It also addresses the limitations of classical cost optimization which could get stuck in local minima due to the multimodality of the path distribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the ability to bridge the gap between purely learning-based and classical navigation approaches. Purely learning-based methods, while performant, often lack generalizability and require extensive training. Purely classical methods, though general, can be brittle and require meticulous hand-tuning. This approach aims for the best of both worlds: a flexible, generalizable learned model that can be guided by explicit, interpretable costs.
    *   The paper demonstrates successful zero-shot transfer, which is highly desirable in robotics. The results show improvements in success rate and collision avoidance compared to strong baselines, particularly in challenging obstacle-rich environments. This underscores the practicality of the approach. The approach could have a significant impact on real-world robotic navigation scenarios where pre-training data is limited and adaptability to dynamic environments is crucial.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Hybrid Approach:** The core strength is the clever combination of learning and explicit cost functions.
    *   **Cost Guidance Integration:** Guiding diffusion model during inference with cost gradients.
    *   **Zero-shot Transfer:** Demonstrated ability to generalize to unseen environments is impressive.
    *   **Strong Experimental Validation:** The paper provides thorough experimental results in both simulated and real-world environments, demonstrating the approach's effectiveness and robustness. The ablation studies clearly show the importance of the cost guidance component.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and the approach is clearly explained. The figures are informative and contribute to the understanding of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Cost Function Design:** The success of the method is still highly dependent on the design of good cost functions (goal reaching and collision avoidance). Although the paper makes reasonable choices, the cost function design could be seen as a form of manual feature engineering, which partially undermines the "end-to-end" spirit of learning-based methods. While the cost function design process is not trivial, the intelligent optimization of costs needs further exploration.
    *   **Limited exploration of high-level policies:** While the introduction mentions integrating the approach with high-level planning (e.g. topological maps) for long-horizon navigation, the paper does not provide sufficient detail or experiments on this aspect. The topological graph is only used to provide subgoals. A deeper integration and analysis of the high-level planning would strengthen the contribution.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models, even with relatively few denoising steps, can be computationally expensive, especially compared to classical planning algorithms. The paper doesn't thoroughly address the computational overhead and real-time performance trade-offs. The complexity in this paper is less because the model is only generating the path, but still, this needs to be taken into consideration.
    *   **Real-world limitations**: The real-world experiments use the same camera and set-up as the simulation. Further real-world experiments under more challenging conditions are required.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the direction of research in visual navigation by providing a compelling alternative to purely learning-based or classical approaches. It demonstrates the potential of combining these paradigms to achieve more robust, generalizable, and interpretable navigation systems.  Future work might build upon this approach by exploring more sophisticated cost functions, learning cost functions directly from data, or improving the efficiency of the diffusion model inference.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed method for visual navigation that effectively combines learning-based and classical approaches.  The cost-guided diffusion model shows significant improvements over baselines in challenging environments and demonstrates impressive zero-shot transfer capabilities. The experimental evaluation is thorough and supports the claims made in the paper. The approach mitigates the shortcomings of learning based and classical planning approaches by combining them in a meaningful way.

However, the reliance on hand-crafted cost functions is a notable limitation, which suggests that it is not truly end-to-end. Moreover, limited experimental data in a real-world environment also needs to be considered.

Despite these limitations, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field of visual navigation and has the potential to influence future research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training LLMs on HPC Systems: Best Practices from the OpenGPT-X Project
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10013v1)
- **Authors**: Carolin Penke, Chelsea Maria John, Jan Ebert, Stefan Kesselheim, Andreas Herten
- **Abstract**: The training of large language models (LLMs) requires substantial computational resources, complex software stacks, and carefully designed workflows to achieve scalability and efficiency. This report presents best practices and insights gained from the OpenGPT-X project, a German initiative focused on developing open, multilingual LLMs optimized for European languages. We detail the use of high-performance computing (HPC) systems, primarily JUWELS Booster at JSC, for training Teuken-7B, a 7-billion-parameter transformer model. The report covers system architecture, training infrastructure, software choices, profiling and benchmarking tools, as well as engineering and operational challenges.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper draft:

**Summary:**

The paper outlines the experiences and best practices gained from the OpenGPT-X project in training large language models (LLMs) on high-performance computing (HPC) systems, specifically the JUWELS Booster at the Jülich Supercomputing Centre.  It covers aspects like choosing a codebase (Megatron-DeepSpeed and later Megatron-LM), setting up the software environment, managing data storage, addressing hardware and software challenges encountered during training, and profiling performance. The paper highlights the importance of collaboration, understanding the system architecture, and adapting existing tools and codebases for effective LLM training on HPC infrastructure. It shares specific environment settings and debugging strategies used during the process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is not in proposing fundamentally new algorithms or models. Instead, it focuses on practical experiences and engineering insights gained from training LLMs on specific HPC infrastructure. While individual aspects (like the resolution of link-flipping or specific software configurations) might be novel to the OpenGPT-X project, the overarching approach of adapting existing LLM training pipelines to HPC environments is a relatively well-explored area.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in documenting and sharing the challenges and solutions encountered during a large-scale project like OpenGPT-X. This is valuable for other researchers and engineers attempting similar tasks, especially those working with similar HPC infrastructure. The detailed account of hardware and software-related issues, profiling techniques, and environment configurations provides a practical guide. However, the paper's focus on a particular system (JUWELS Booster) may limit its generalizability to other HPC environments.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Focus:** It's a hands-on account of real-world challenges and solutions.
    *   **Detailed Information:** Specific environment variables, debugging tips, and configuration details are provided.
    *   **HPC Specific:** The paper caters to HPC, which is a different field than just straight ML.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The focus on JUWELS Booster limits the applicability to other HPC systems.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:**  The paper describes adaptations and optimizations of existing techniques rather than introducing groundbreaking innovations.
