### MSCoT: Structured Chain-of-Thought Generation for Multiple Programming Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10178v1)
- **Authors**: Naizhu Jin, Zhong Li, Tian Zhang, Qingkai Zeng
- **Abstract**: With the rapid development of code intelligence, the application of multiple programming languages is becoming increasingly widespread. However, most existing code generation models mainly focus on a single or a few programming languages, resulting in unsatisfactory performance in a multilingual environment. Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning can significantly improve the performance of the model without the need for retraining or fine-tuning the code generation model by reasonably decomposing complex code generation tasks into multiple subtasks and gradually deriving solutions for each subtask. Nevertheless, the existing CoT generation methods mainly concentrate on Python code, and the performance on other programming languages remains unclear. To fill this gap, we first constructed a CoT generation dataset for 12 programming languages through multi-agent technology. On this basis, we proposed a CoT generation method MSCoT applicable to multiple programming languages. By introducing CoT into the code generation large model, the performance of the code generation large model in a multilingual environment can be improved. Through large-scale empirical research, we compared the generalization abilities of MSCoT and the existing CoT generation methods on multiple programming languages and proved the effectiveness of MSCoT for multiple programming languages. In addition, we also designed a human study to prove the quality of the CoT generated by MSCoT. Finally, we opensourced the model and dataset of MSCoT to promote the research on CoT generation for multiple programming languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MSCOT: Structured Chain-of-Thought Generation for Multiple Programming Languages" addresses the limitations of existing code generation models that primarily focus on single or a few programming languages, which leads to suboptimal performance in multilingual environments. The authors introduce MSCOT, a structured chain-of-thought (CoT) generation method applicable to 12 programming languages. They constructed a large-scale CoT dataset using a multi-agent framework involving Code Quality Agent (CQAgent), Code Translation Agent (CTAgent), and Structured Chain-of-Thought Agent (SCoTAgent). They fine-tuned the Qwen2.5Coder model using LoRA and introduced an instruction template to guide LLMs in generating structured CoT explanations. The experiments demonstrate that MSCOT improves the performance of code generation models across multiple programming languages, with results comparable to GPT-4-based solutions while using fewer computational resources. A human study validates the quality of the generated CoTs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in creating a large-scale CoT dataset for 12 programming languages using a well-designed multi-agent system. The instruction template to guide LLMs in generating structured CoT explanations is also a valuable contribution. Fine-tuning a smaller model (Qwen2.5Coder) using LoRA is a practical approach for resource-constrained scenarios, but LoRA itself is not new. The idea of structured CoT, while leveraging the SCoT framework, is built upon prior work.

*   **Significance:**  The paper tackles a relevant and increasingly important problem: code generation in multilingual environments. As software development becomes more complex and diverse, supporting multiple languages is crucial. The demonstrated improvements in code generation performance using MSCOT are significant. The human evaluation further strengthens the findings by demonstrating the usefulness of the generated CoTs for developers. The open-sourcing of the model and dataset promises to foster further research in this area. However, the significance is somewhat mitigated by the fact that the architectural contributions of the proposed method rely heavily on established chain-of-thought techniques and the utilization of existing LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive dataset creation for multiple programming languages.
    *   Well-designed multi-agent framework for data generation.
    *   Effective fine-tuning using LoRA for resource-constrained environments.
    *   Detailed experimental evaluation and analysis.
    *   Validation through human study.
    *   Open-sourcing of the model and dataset.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The core technical approach relies heavily on existing CoT techniques and does not introduce significant architectural innovations beyond SCoT.
    *   While impressive, the relative performance improvements may not be sufficient to fully close the gap with much larger, closed-source models like GPT-4.
    *   The human evaluation relies on a limited number of participants for each language, which may impact the generalizability of the findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** The MSCOT dataset and the insights provided by the paper can serve as a valuable resource for researchers working on multilingual code generation and CoT reasoning. The work promotes the development of more accessible and efficient code generation models for resource-constrained environments.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of multilingual code generation by addressing the limitations of existing models and providing a practical and effective approach for generating CoT explanations. The construction of the dataset and the insights from the experimental evaluation are significant. However, the technical innovation beyond the dataset construction is incremental. The work builds upon established CoT techniques and does not introduce substantial architectural advancements. Furthermore, the use of LLMs might be a limitation for future work, as it relies on models from others and the reproducibility is subject to the availability of these models. While valuable and impactful, the contribution does not represent a paradigm shift.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Future of MLLM Prompting is Adaptive: A Comprehensive Experimental Evaluation of Prompt Engineering Methods for Robust Multimodal Performance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10179v1)
- **Authors**: Anwesha Mohanty, Venkatesh Balavadhani Parthasarathy, Arsalan Shahid
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are set to transform how machines process and generate human-like responses by integrating diverse modalities such as text, images, and code. Yet, effectively harnessing their capabilities hinges on optimal prompt engineering. We present a comprehensive experimental evaluation of seven prompt engineering methods applied to 13 open-source MLLMs over 24 tasks spanning Reasoning and Compositionality, Multimodal Understanding and Alignment, Complex Code Generation and Execution, and Knowledge Retrieval and Integration. Our approach stratifies models by parameter count into Small (<4B), Medium (4B-10B), and Large (>10B) categories and compares prompting techniques including Zero-Shot, One-Shot, Few-Shot, Chain-of-Thought, Analogical, Generated Knowledge, and Tree-of-Thought. While Large MLLMs excel in structured tasks such as code generation, achieving accuracies up to 96.88% under Few-Shot prompting, all models struggle with complex reasoning and abstract understanding, often yielding accuracies below 60% and high hallucination rates. Structured reasoning prompts frequently increased hallucination up to 75% in small models and led to longer response times (over 20 seconds in Large MLLMs), while simpler prompting methods provided more concise and efficient outputs. No single prompting method uniformly optimises all task types. Instead, adaptive strategies combining example-based guidance with selective structured reasoning are essential to enhance robustness, efficiency, and factual accuracy. Our findings offer practical recommendations for prompt engineering and support more reliable deployment of MLLMs across applications including AI-assisted coding, knowledge retrieval, and multimodal content understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive experimental evaluation of prompt engineering methods for multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It explores seven different prompting techniques (Zero-Shot, One-Shot, Few-Shot, Chain-of-Thought, Analogical, Generated Knowledge, and Tree-of-Thought) across 13 open-source MLLMs and 24 tasks, covering Reasoning and Compositionality, Multimodal Understanding and Alignment, Code Generation and Execution, and Knowledge Retrieval and Integration. The authors stratify models by parameter count and assess the impact of each prompting method on accuracy, relevancy, conciseness, hallucination rates, inference time, and memory consumption. The key finding is that no single prompting method optimizes performance across all task types; instead, adaptive prompting strategies combining example-based guidance and selective structured reasoning are crucial for robustness and efficiency. The paper concludes with actionable recommendations for optimizing prompt engineering in real-world MLLM applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Experimental Design:** The paper undertakes a large-scale empirical study, systematically comparing multiple prompting methods across a diverse set of MLLMs and tasks. This breadth contributes significantly to the field by providing a detailed understanding of how different prompts interact with models of varying sizes and architectures.
    *   **Emphasis on Practical Metrics:** The inclusion of metrics beyond accuracy, such as hallucination rate, inference time, and memory consumption, demonstrates a practical focus on real-world deployment considerations. The results offer valuable insights into the trade-offs between performance and resource efficiency.
    *   **Actionable Recommendations:** The paper goes beyond simply presenting results and offers concrete guidance for choosing the most appropriate prompting strategy for different applications. The emphasis on adaptive prompting is particularly valuable.
    *   **Open-Source Focus:** The choice to evaluate open-source models increases the impact and accessibility of the research, facilitating further studies and enabling wider adoption of the findings.
    *   **Addresses a Critical Gap:** By focusing on prompt engineering for *multimodal* LLMs, the study fills a gap in the existing literature, which often concentrates on text-only models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Prompting Techniques:** The prompting methods themselves (e.g., Chain-of-Thought, Few-Shot) are well-established in the field of LLMs. The novelty lies primarily in their application and systematic evaluation within the multimodal context.
    *   **Task Selection:** While the tasks are diverse, some tasks can be more synthetic and academic and not directly translate to complex real-world tasks. Further justification for the ecological validity of certain tasks would strengthen the results.
    *   **Metric Definition:**  The descriptions of how "relevancy" and "conciseness" were assessed and measured could benefit from more concrete details.  Providing specific examples of how these metrics were applied would increase rigor.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** While the paper presents a quantitative analysis, it could be strengthened by including more qualitative examples of model outputs, illustrating the nuances of the different prompting methods and task types. This could offer deeper insights into the models' reasoning processes and failure modes.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Hybrid Approaches:** While the paper advocates for adaptive prompting, it does not deeply explore or evaluate specific hybrid strategies. Future work could focus on systematically designing and testing such approaches.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper addresses a significant need in the rapidly evolving field of MLLMs: effective prompt engineering. By providing a comprehensive empirical analysis and practical recommendations, it contributes to a better understanding of how to harness the potential of these models in real-world applications. The findings have the potential to influence the design and deployment of MLLM-based systems in domains ranging from AI-assisted coding to knowledge retrieval. The emphasis on mitigating hallucination and optimizing resource consumption is particularly important for enabling responsible and efficient use of MLLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is well-written, comprehensive, and tackles a practically relevant problem. While the individual prompting techniques are not novel, the systematic evaluation across diverse MLLMs, task types, and practical metrics (hallucination, resource usage) offers a significant contribution to the field. The study provides actionable recommendations for MLLM deployment. However, the limited exploration of hybrid prompting strategies and reliance on some synthetic tasks (rather than exclusively real-world) are weaknesses. These trade-offs, when taken together, merit a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Generative Model Training via Embedded Representation Warmup
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10188v1)
- **Authors**: Deyuan Liu, Peng Sun, Xufeng Li, Tao Lin
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models excel at generating high-dimensional data but fall short in training efficiency and representation quality compared to self-supervised methods. We identify a key bottleneck: the underutilization of high-quality, semantically rich representations during training notably slows down convergence. Our systematic analysis reveals a critical representation processing region -- primarily in the early layers -- where semantic and structural pattern learning takes place before generation can occur. To address this, we propose Embedded Representation Warmup (ERW), a plug-and-play framework where in the first stage we get the ERW module serves as a warmup that initializes the early layers of the diffusion model with high-quality, pretrained representations. This warmup minimizes the burden of learning representations from scratch, thereby accelerating convergence and boosting performance. Our theoretical analysis demonstrates that ERW's efficacy depends on its precise integration into specific neural network layers -- termed the representation processing region -- where the model primarily processes and transforms feature representations for later generation. We further establish that ERW not only accelerates training convergence but also enhances representation quality: empirically, our method achieves a 40$\times$ acceleration in training speed compared to REPA, the current state-of-the-art methods. Code is available at https://github.com/LINs-lab/ERW.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Embedded Representation Warmup (ERW), a novel two-phase training framework for diffusion models aimed at improving training efficiency and representation quality. ERW decouples representation learning from generative decoding. In the first phase, the early layers of the diffusion model are initialized with weights from a pre-trained self-supervised model (e.g., DINOv2) using a representation alignment loss. This step injects strong semantic priors. In the second phase, the model transitions to standard diffusion training with a gradually diminishing alignment term. The paper argues that this separation allows the model to focus on generative capabilities after learning a strong initial representation. Experiments demonstrate significant speedups (up to 40x compared to REPA) while maintaining state-of-the-art generative quality. The authors theoretically analyze and empirically validate the importance of initializing specific early layers related to representation processing. They also conduct ablation studies to analyze the effects of various alignment strategies, architecture depths, and target representation models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of decoupling representation learning and generative decoding for diffusion models is a worthwhile exploration.  The ERW framework itself is relatively novel.  While prior work has explored injecting pre-trained representations or using external encoders with diffusion models (e.g., REPA, Würstchen), ERW's explicit two-phase approach, and the focus on warming up specific early layers based on analysis of the network's function are unique. The detailed ablation studies on layer depth, architecture, and the decomposition of the diffusion process into latent-to-representation (L2R) and representation-to-generation (R2G) circuits offer new insights.
*   **Significance:**  The potential impact of ERW is substantial.  Diffusion models, despite their impressive generative capabilities, are computationally expensive to train. A 40x speedup without sacrificing quality would be a significant advancement.  By improving training efficiency, ERW could enable the development and deployment of larger, more sophisticated generative models.  The insights into the role of specific network layers in representation learning and generation contribute to a better understanding of how diffusion models operate. This improved understanding facilitates better design choices for more efficient architectures.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the ERW framework and its underlying motivation.
    *   The experimental results are convincing, demonstrating consistent improvements in training efficiency and generation quality across various settings. The thorough ablation studies offer valuable insights into the design choices.
    *   The theoretical analysis provides a solid foundation for the ERW approach and helps to explain why it works. The partitioning of the workflow into two distinct circuits adds to the understanding of the process.
    *   The practical results, like reaching a strong FID score on ImageNet in under 4 hours using readily available hardware, are significant.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty, while present, is incremental. It builds upon existing work on injecting pre-trained representations.
    *   The dependence on a pre-trained self-supervised model may limit ERW's applicability to domains where such models are not readily available or perform poorly. The paper might benefit from discussing approaches to mitigating this limitation (e.g., training a self-supervised model jointly with the diffusion model).
    *  The practical gains are primarily benchmarked on the ImageNet dataset. While ImageNet is a standard benchmark, the authors should demonstrate this strategy's applicability across a broader range of datasets to fortify the robustness of the strategy.
*   **Justification:** The paper presents a valuable technique for improving the training efficiency of diffusion models. The explicit decoupling of representation learning and generative decoding, combined with the detailed analysis of network layer roles, contributes meaningfully to the field. While the core idea builds upon prior work, the specific implementation and insights are novel and have the potential to influence the design of future diffusion models. The potential for substantially faster training times is extremely valuable.

**Score: 7.5**

The paper is technically sound, experimentally validated, and offers practical benefits. The improvement in training efficiency has significant implications. However, the reliance on pre-trained self-supervised models and incremental nature of the novelty prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Localized Cultural Knowledge is Conserved and Controllable in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10191v1)
- **Authors**: Veniamin Veselovsky, Berke Argin, Benedikt Stroebl, Chris Wendler, Robert West, James Evans, Thomas L. Griffiths, Arvind Narayanan
- **Abstract**: Just as humans display language patterns influenced by their native tongue when speaking new languages, LLMs often default to English-centric responses even when generating in other languages. Nevertheless, we observe that local cultural information persists within the models and can be readily activated for cultural customization. We first demonstrate that explicitly providing cultural context in prompts significantly improves the models' ability to generate culturally localized responses. We term the disparity in model performance with versus without explicit cultural context the explicit-implicit localization gap, indicating that while cultural knowledge exists within LLMs, it may not naturally surface in multilingual interactions if cultural context is not explicitly provided. Despite the explicit prompting benefit, however, the answers reduce in diversity and tend toward stereotypes. Second, we identify an explicit cultural customization vector, conserved across all non-English languages we explore, which enables LLMs to be steered from the synthetic English cultural world-model toward each non-English cultural world. Steered responses retain the diversity of implicit prompting and reduce stereotypes to dramatically improve the potential for customization. We discuss the implications of explicit cultural customization for understanding the conservation of alternative cultural world models within LLMs, and their controllable utility for translation, cultural customization, and the possibility of making the explicit implicit through soft control for expanded LLM function and appeal.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how localized cultural knowledge is conserved and controllable within large language models (LLMs).  It introduces the concept of the "explicit-implicit localization gap," the difference in LLM performance when cultural context is explicitly provided in the prompt versus when it's only implied through the language of the prompt.  The authors demonstrate that LLMs often default to English-centric responses in multilingual interactions, but that cultural knowledge exists within the models and can be activated through explicit prompting. While explicit prompting improves performance on cultural tasks, it also reduces the diversity of responses and increases stereotypicality. To address this, the authors use mechanistic interpretability techniques to identify and create "cultural steering vectors" that can be applied to model activations to encourage culturally localized responses without the need for explicit cultural prompts.  The steered responses maintain diversity, reduce stereotypes, and better align with the underlying culture. Finally, the paper explores the potential for a universal cultural localization vector.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of the explicit-implicit localization gap is a valuable framing. While prior work has explored cultural bias in LLMs, this paper provides a more nuanced perspective by distinguishing between the *existence* of cultural knowledge and its *activation*. The use of mechanistic interpretability to derive steering vectors is a strong point, building on established techniques but applying them in a novel way to a practical cultural understanding problem. However, the basic finding that explicit context improves LLM behavior is not novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have implications for building more culturally aware and appropriate AI systems. The steering vector approach is particularly significant because it offers a path towards controllable cultural customization, overcoming the limitations of simple prompting and fine-tuning. By moving towards model steering based on culture, the research opens the door to more globally appropriate and acceptable responses from language models. The work helps contextualize existing cultural benchmarks.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-defined methodology.
    *   The introduction of the explicit-implicit localization gap as a metric.
    *   The successful demonstration of cultural steering vectors and their ability to generalize across languages and tasks.
    *   Rigorous experimentation with multiple models, languages, and tasks.
    *   The analysis of stereotypicality and faithfulness, going beyond simple accuracy metrics.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The scope is limited to five languages. Extending the analysis to a broader range of linguistic and cultural settings would strengthen the findings.
    *   The reliance on specific prompt designs is a potential limitation. The generalizability of the steering vectors to different prompt formulations should be further investigated.
    *   The evaluation of the "universal culture vector" is preliminary and requires further investigation. The construction of this vector is limited by a binary choice architecture.
    *   The reliance on a single base model. While the approach works, there is significant need for further study to understand if the approach extends to models of varying architectures.
    *   Downstream effects and potential for harmful outcomes in high-stakes use cases is not addressed.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper's approach to cultural steering could be a key component in building more culturally sensitive AI applications, particularly in translation, chatbots, and content generation. The work encourages future research into more effective and controllable methods for cultural adaptation in LLMs. The work suggests that there is a way to embed culture and identity in these models that goes beyond explicit prompting, helping further increase their applicability to the full scope of people they serve.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's strengths in its novel framing, methodological rigor, and the potential of its proposed steering vector approach outweigh its limitations. It builds on existing work, contributes a valuable new perspective and opens up avenues for future research in a critical area of AI development. Though the analysis is not perfect, the approach and direction of the work is noteworthy.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DioR: Adaptive Cognitive Detection and Contextual Retrieval Optimization for Dynamic Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10198v1)
- **Authors**: Hanghui Guo, Jia Zhu, Shimin Di, Weijie Shi, Zhangze Chen, Jiajie Xu
- **Abstract**: Dynamic Retrieval-augmented Generation (RAG) has shown great success in mitigating hallucinations in large language models (LLMs) during generation. However, existing dynamic RAG methods face significant limitations in two key aspects: 1) Lack of an effective mechanism to control retrieval triggers, and 2) Lack of effective scrutiny of retrieval content. To address these limitations, we propose an innovative dynamic RAG method, DioR (Adaptive Cognitive Detection and Contextual Retrieval Optimization), which consists of two main components: adaptive cognitive detection and contextual retrieval optimization, specifically designed to determine when retrieval is needed and what to retrieve for LLMs is useful. Experimental results demonstrate that DioR achieves superior performance on all tasks, demonstrating the effectiveness of our work.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DioR: Adaptive Cognitive Detection and Contextual Retrieval Optimization for Dynamic Retrieval-Augmented Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DioR, a novel dynamic Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) method designed to mitigate hallucinations in large language models (LLMs). DioR addresses two key limitations of existing dynamic RAG approaches: the lack of effective mechanisms to control retrieval triggers, and the absence of effective scrutiny of retrieved content. DioR achieves this through two main components:
1.  **Adaptive Cognitive Detection:** Classifies early & real-time hallucinations. Early Detection assesses the LLM's ability to answer questions independently, while Real-time Detection monitors for hallucinations during generation.
2.  **Contextual Retrieval Optimization:** Contextual retrieval optimization uses a step-by-step document retrieving process using a query priority keyword optimizing.

The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that DioR outperforms existing methods on several knowledge-intensive generation datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses Important Limitations:** The paper tackles significant and well-recognized problems in dynamic RAG. The limitations around trigger control and content scrutiny are valid and practical.
*   **Sound methodology:** The architecture of DiOR is methodologically sound, and is structured in a way where each sub-component serves a purpose in resolving dynamic RAG related issues.
*   **Clear structure and presentation:** The paper is reasonably well-structured and the methodology is explained clearly, making it relatively easy to understand the proposed approach.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the paper combines multiple existing techniques, its novelty is somewhat incremental. The integration of classifiers for hallucination detection and contextual retrieval optimization is not groundbreaking, but rather a practical application of well-established methods.
*   **Reliance on Existing Methods:** DioR heavily depends on existing techniques like Integrated Gradients, BM25, SBERT, and GPT for feature extraction, embedding, and retrieval. The paper doesn't introduce fundamentally new algorithms or theoretical insights in these areas.
*   **Limited Ablation Study:** The ablation study, while present, could be more thorough. For example, it is unclear how the specific weights chosen for the combinations of the four evaluation metrics in section 3.3.1 affect the final performance.

**Significance and Impact:**

*   **Practical Contribution:** DioR offers a practical solution that can be readily implemented and deployed to improve the reliability of LLM-based systems. This makes it a valuable contribution for practitioners in the field.
*   **Potential for Further Research:** The work can serve as a basis for further research into more sophisticated methods for hallucination detection and contextual retrieval. The results may encourage other researchers to explore similar hybrid approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a useful contribution to the field of dynamic RAG by combining existing techniques in a novel and practical way to address critical limitations. While the novelty is not exceptionally high, the empirical results and the clear methodology justify a solid score. The limitations in the ablation study and over-reliance on existing methods prevent it from scoring higher. Overall, the practical contribution and the potential for further research make it a worthwhile addition to the literature.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Can Competition Enhance the Proficiency of Agents Powered by Large Language Models in the Realm of News-driven Time Series Forecasting?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10210v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Zhang, Yangyang Feng, Daifeng Li, Kexin Zhang, Junlan Chen, Bowen Deng
- **Abstract**: Multi-agents-based news-driven time series forecasting is considered as a potential paradigm shift in the era of large language models (LLMs). The challenge of this task lies in measuring the influences of different news events towards the fluctuations of time series. This requires agents to possess stronger abilities of innovative thinking and the identifying misleading logic. However, the existing multi-agent discussion framework has limited enhancement on time series prediction in terms of optimizing these two capabilities. Inspired by the role of competition in fostering innovation, this study embeds a competition mechanism within the multi-agent discussion to enhance agents' capability of generating innovative thoughts. Furthermore, to bolster the model's proficiency in identifying misleading information, we incorporate a fine-tuned small-scale LLM model within the reflective stage, offering auxiliary decision-making support. Experimental results confirm that the competition can boost agents' capacity for innovative thinking, which can significantly improve the performances of time series prediction. Similar to the findings of social science, the intensity of competition within this framework can influence the performances of agents, providing a new perspective for studying LLMs-based multi-agent systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether introducing competition among Large Language Model (LLM)-powered agents enhances their performance in news-driven time series forecasting.  It proposes a framework that integrates a competition mechanism (inspired by the stock market), including Information Asymmetry, Competitive Awareness, and Survival of the Fittest, within a multi-agent discussion framework.  The goal is to stimulate innovative thinking and mitigate the "Degeneration of Thought" (DoT) problem commonly observed in collaborative LLM settings. Additionally, the paper introduces a Multi-stage Reflection (MSR) module, leveraging a fine-tuned small-scale LLM, to improve agents' ability to identify misleading information.  The study presents experimental results demonstrating that the competition mechanism improves time series prediction and analyzes the impact of competition intensity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the introduction of a *competition* mechanism into a multi-agent LLM forecasting setting. While multi-agent systems and LLMs for time series have been explored, the use of game-theoretic concepts and the attempt to explicitly address the DoT problem by fostering competition are relatively new.  The MSR module, while leveraging existing techniques (fine-tuning), represents a practical engineering solution to improve robustness in the competitive context. However, competitive multi-agent scenarios have been studied in the context of social simulations, so the novelty is primarily in applying this to time-series forecasting.

*   **Significance:** Time series forecasting is a crucial task across many domains. Improving the accuracy and robustness of such systems using LLMs is a valuable goal. The paper's findings suggesting that competition can indeed enhance innovative thinking among agents and improve forecasting performance are potentially significant. It provides a novel perspective for optimizing LLM-based multi-agent systems, particularly by countering the groupthink tendencies of collaborative approaches. The analysis of competition intensity's influence, drawing parallels to social science findings, is also a promising direction.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges of news-driven time series forecasting using LLMs and the limitations of existing multi-agent discussion frameworks.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:**  The rationale for using competition is well-grounded in the theory of innovation and stock market dynamics.
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The proposed framework, incorporating competition, Information Asymmetry, and the MSR module, is well-designed.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents experimental results that support its claims, including comparisons against strong baselines and ablation studies to assess the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Analysis of Competition Intensity:**  The U-shaped correlation analysis offers valuable insights into the optimal level of competition for performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset Variety:** The experiments rely on a limited set of four datasets from a single source. Expanding the evaluation to more diverse and complex time series datasets would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Dependence on Prompt Engineering:** Like many LLM-based approaches, the performance of the framework is likely sensitive to prompt design. While the paper discusses prompt settings and provides paraphrased versions, more analysis of prompt robustness is needed.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges the high computational resources required. Exploring ways to optimize the framework for greater efficiency would enhance its practicality.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Agent Behaviors:** While performance is analyzed, deeper understanding of the specific logic created by different agents in response to competition would enrich the insights. Qualitative analyses of agent behaviors would further support claims about innovative thinking.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the findings can be replicated across diverse datasets and if the computational cost can be reduced, the paper's framework could influence the design of future LLM-based time series forecasting systems. It might inspire further research into the application of game-theoretic mechanisms to multi-agent LLM systems.

**Overall:** The paper introduces a novel and potentially significant approach to news-driven time series forecasting by integrating a competition mechanism into a multi-agent LLM framework. The experimental results are promising, but further validation and analysis are needed to address the limitations.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel approach to a significant problem, backed by empirical evidence. While there are weaknesses related to dataset diversity, prompt dependence, and computational cost, the paper offers valuable insights and contributes a new perspective to the field. Therefore, it doesn't reach the level of a groundbreaking discovery (8-9) but represents a solid and promising contribution above average (6), meriting the score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### PRM-BAS: Enhancing Multimodal Reasoning through PRM-guided Beam Annealing Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10222v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Hu, Zhenrong Zhang, Qikai Chang, Shuhang Liu, Jiefeng Ma, Jun Du, Jianshu Zhang, Quan Liu, Jianqing Gao, Feng Ma, Qingfeng Liu
- **Abstract**: Recent work increasingly focuses on improving the reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Among existing methods, Process Reward Models (PRMs) stand out for offering dense, step-wise supervision to guide intermediate reasoning. However, how to effectively integrate PRMs into search strategies remains an open question. In this paper, we introduce PRM-BAS (PRM-Guided Beam Annealing Search), a lightweight approach for PRM-guided reasoning that dynamically adjusts beam size -- starting with a broader search space and gradually narrowing it as contextual information accumulates, thereby balancing performance and efficiency. We further propose a unified framework for data construction and PRM training. Specifically, we construct the PRM-BAS-300k dataset by selecting 300k questions from existing datasets and performing rollouts at each step to estimate the probability of reaching a correct final answer. The PRM is then trained using a combination of value loss for absolute action quality and rank loss for relative action quality. Extensive experiments on challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that PRM-BAS significantly improves reasoning performance while maintaining low computational cost. Moreover, it generalizes well across different model scales and architectures, showcasing strong robustness and plug-and-play capability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PRM-BAS (PRM-Guided Beam Annealing Search), a novel approach designed to enhance the reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). PRM-BAS uses Process Reward Models (PRMs) to guide a dynamic beam search strategy. The beam size starts large to explore different reasoning paths and is then gradually reduced as more contextual information becomes available, aiming to balance performance and computational efficiency. The authors also present a unified framework for data construction and PRM training, including the PRM-BAS-300k dataset.  Experiments on various multimodal reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that PRM-BAS improves reasoning performance while maintaining low computational cost and exhibits good generalization across different model scales and architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the combination of PRMs with a beam annealing search strategy. While PRMs themselves are not entirely new, their integration with a dynamically adjusted beam size offers a unique contribution. The unified framework for data creation and PRM training, particularly the rollout-based step sampling, also presents a novel aspect. The approach is lightweight and aims to address some limitations of existing methods like BoN and MCTS, which suffer from delayed feedback or high computational overhead.
* **Significance:**  The paper demonstrates promising results on several challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks. The improvement in reasoning performance while keeping computational costs down is significant, particularly for real-world applications where resources may be limited. The claimed generalization across different model scales and architectures is another valuable contribution. By showing a relatively lightweight technique can boost existing model performance, the paper opens up potential avenues for improving MLLMs without extensive retraining or parameter updates.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges of PRM-guided reasoning, including the need for efficient search strategies and effective data construction/training methods.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The proposed PRM-BAS strategy is well-motivated by the analysis of reward dynamics at different reasoning stages.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents extensive experimental results on a wide range of datasets, comparing against several baselines and state-of-the-art models. The ablation study provides further insights into the effectiveness of different components of the proposed framework.
    *   **Lightweight Nature:** By maintaining low computational cost, the method makes a significant contribution by making PRMs much more practical.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Policy-Dependence Issue:** While the paper acknowledges and discusses the Policy-Dependent Issue, it remains a limitation. The reliance on consistency between training and inference policy models could hinder broader applicability.
    *   **Model Size Limitations:** The experiments are primarily conducted on relatively smaller models (up to 8B parameters). While the paper claims generalization across different scales, it would be valuable to see how PRM-BAS performs on larger models (e.g., 32B or 72B). This is a major constraint to its significance.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of ideas is novel, it's also somewhat incremental. PRMs and beam search were already common and the specific design of the annealing could be viewed as a straightforward extension.
    *   **Missing code release**: Not releasing code makes it difficult to verify findings.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in multimodal reasoning by:
    *   Encouraging the development of more efficient and lightweight techniques for PRM-guided reasoning.
    *   Providing a useful framework for data construction and PRM training.
    *   Inspiring further exploration of dynamic search strategies for MLLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. PRM-BAS is a well-executed and promising method for improving multimodal reasoning. The paper makes a novel contribution by combining PRMs with a dynamic beam search strategy and presents compelling experimental results. The lightweight nature of the approach and the unified framework for data construction/training are valuable aspects. However, the Policy-Dependent Issue and the lack of experiments on larger models limit its potential influence. The lack of code release also limits future use of this method. Given these considerations, while PRM-BAS is a solid contribution, it does not reach the level of a truly groundbreaking paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.MM
- **Score**: 7/10

### Probing then Editing Response Personality of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10227v1)
- **Authors**: Tianjie Ju, Zhenyu Shao, Bowen Wang, Yujia Chen, Zhuosheng Zhang, Hao Fei, Mong-Li Lee, Wynne Hsu, Sufeng Duan, Gongshen Liu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated promising capabilities to generate responses that exhibit consistent personality traits. Despite the major attempts to analyze personality expression through output-based evaluations, little is known about how such traits are internally encoded within LLM parameters. In this paper, we introduce a layer-wise probing framework to systematically investigate the layer-wise capability of LLMs in encoding personality for responding. We conduct probing experiments on 11 open-source LLMs over the PersonalityEdit benchmark and find that LLMs predominantly encode personality for responding in their middle and upper layers, with instruction-tuned models demonstrating a slightly clearer separation of personality traits. Furthermore, by interpreting the trained probing hyperplane as a layer-wise boundary for each personality category, we propose a layer-wise perturbation method to edit the personality expressed by LLMs during inference. Our results show that even when the prompt explicitly specifies a particular personality, our method can still successfully alter the response personality of LLMs. Interestingly, the difficulty of converting between certain personality traits varies substantially, which aligns with the representational distances in our probing experiments. Finally, we conduct a comprehensive MMLU benchmark evaluation and time overhead analysis, demonstrating that our proposed personality editing method incurs only minimal degradation in general capabilities while maintaining low training costs and acceptable inference latency. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/universe-sky/probing-then-editing-personality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Probing then Editing Response Personality of Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a framework for analyzing and manipulating the personality expressed by Large Language Models (LLMs) in their responses. It first uses a layer-wise probing technique to identify where personality-related information is encoded within the LLM's parameters.  It trains classifiers at each layer to predict the personality trait exhibited in a response, using the hidden representations as input. This probing reveals that personality encoding primarily occurs in the middle and upper layers.  Then, it leverages these trained classifiers to edit the LLM's behavior at inference time. By perturbing the hidden states of the LLM in a direction orthogonal to the learned classification hyperplanes, the method steers the LLM towards a desired personality, even when the prompt specifies a different personality. The paper evaluates the method on the PersonalityEdit benchmark, demonstrating successful personality editing with minimal degradation in general capabilities and acceptable computational overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach to understanding and controlling LLM personality. Layer-wise probing for personality encoding is a good contribution. The idea of using probing classifiers to *edit* LLM behavior, rather than just analyze it, is also significant. It is related to work in interventions and "steering" LLMs, but the application to personality is fairly novel. Compared to previous attempts that heavily rely on prompting or fine-tuning to change LLM personality, the current approach demonstrates a good balance between controlling personality aspects while maintaining the model's original capacity.

*   **Significance:** This work is significant for several reasons:
    *   It sheds light on how personality is encoded within LLMs, moving beyond black-box evaluations.
    *   It provides a method for editing LLM personality without requiring large-scale annotated datasets or extensive retraining, which reduces the reliance on labeled examples.
    *   It offers a way to make LLMs more adaptable and controllable in diverse applications, making them behave in certain ways to elicit desired responses. For instance, adapting the "persona" of LLMs would enhance user engagement.
    *   The study reveals that certain personality trait conversions are more difficult than others, which may suggest that personality traits are organized differently within the LLMs parameter space. The framework could be expanded to more dimensions other than personality, which has profound implications for the explainability and reliability of the LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, including a layer-wise probing analysis, personality editing experiments, and evaluations of general capabilities and time overhead.
    *   The results are compelling, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    *   The comparison with existing baselines is insightful, highlighting the advantages of the proposed approach.
    *   The code is publicly available, which supports reproducibility and facilitates future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on linear probing, while computationally efficient, might oversimplify the complex, non-linear relationships within LLM representations. This could lead to an incomplete understanding of how personality is truly encoded.
    *   The evaluation is limited to the PersonalityEdit benchmark. Expanding the evaluation to other datasets and tasks related to personality expression could further strengthen the findings.
    *   The study focuses primarily on three personality traits (Neuroticism, Extraversion, and Agreeableness). While these are important, a broader exploration of other personality dimensions (e.g., conscientiousness, openness) could provide a more comprehensive picture.
    *   The paper does not explore the potential ethical implications of personality editing in detail. It's important to consider the risks associated with manipulating user emotions or creating deceptive AI agents.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of LLM research. It opens up new avenues for exploring the internal representations of LLMs and developing methods for controlling their behavior. It could also inspire further research on the ethical implications of AI personality and the development of responsible AI technologies.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the understanding and control of LLM personality. The layer-wise probing and editing framework is well-designed and the experimental evaluation is thorough. While there are some limitations, such as the reliance on linear probing and the limited scope of personality traits, the paper offers valuable insights and has the potential to inspire further research in this area. The combination of probing and editing techniques makes it a significant step forward. The work is clearly explained, well-evaluated, and has significant potential influence.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Model Zoo of Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10231v1)
- **Authors**: Damian Falk, Léo Meynent, Florence Pfammatter, Konstantin Schürholt, Damian Borth
- **Abstract**: The availability of large, structured populations of neural networks - called 'model zoos' - has led to the development of a multitude of downstream tasks ranging from model analysis, to representation learning on model weights or generative modeling of neural network parameters. However, existing model zoos are limited in size and architecture and neglect the transformer, which is among the currently most successful neural network architectures. We address this gap by introducing the first model zoo of vision transformers (ViT). To better represent recent training approaches, we develop a new blueprint for model zoo generation that encompasses both pre-training and fine-tuning steps, and publish 250 unique models. They are carefully generated with a large span of generating factors, and their diversity is validated using a thorough choice of weight-space and behavioral metrics. To further motivate the utility of our proposed dataset, we suggest multiple possible applications grounded in both extensive exploratory experiments and a number of examples from the existing literature. By extending previous lines of similar work, our model zoo allows researchers to push their model population-based methods from the small model regime to state-of-the-art architectures. We make our model zoo available at github.com/ModelZoos/ViTModelZoo.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new "model zoo" consisting of 250 Vision Transformer (ViT-S) models.  It addresses the current lack of transformer-based model zoos, which are crucial for research areas like model analysis, representation learning, and generative modeling of neural network parameters. The model zoo is generated using a two-stage training approach: pre-training on ImageNet-1k followed by fine-tuning on CIFAR-100, with variations in pre-training tasks (supervised and self-supervised) and fine-tuning hyperparameters.  The authors analyze the performance and diversity of the model zoo using weight-space and behavioral metrics. They also explore potential applications, including model lineage prediction and model weight averaging, demonstrating the zoo's utility as a testbed for weight space learning research. The zoo is made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in creating the *first* structured model zoo of Vision Transformers with a two-stage (pre-training and fine-tuning) training paradigm.  Existing model zoos are primarily focused on CNNs and lack the structured variation in training approaches that this paper provides.

*   **Significance:**  Model zoos serve as valuable resources for the machine learning research community.  By providing a structured dataset of ViT models, this paper facilitates research in areas such as:

    *   Understanding the properties of ViTs.
    *   Developing new representation learning methods for model weights.
    *   Investigating model lineage and transfer learning.
    *   Exploring weight averaging techniques.
    *   Generating new ViT weights using generative models.

    This can lead to improvements in model robustness, generalization, and efficiency. The provision of model checkpoints across training epochs further enhances the zoo's utility.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Structured and Reproducible:** The authors formalize the model zoo generation process, making it reproducible.
    *   **Diversity Analysis:** The paper includes a thorough analysis of the diversity within the model zoo using various metrics.
    *   **Potential Applications:**  The authors suggest and explore potential applications, motivating the usefulness of the dataset.
    *   **Public Availability:**  Making the model zoo publicly available increases its impact and enables further research.
    * Two-stage training paradigm is very relevant to transfer learning and not adequately represented in previous model zoos.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Architecture and Datasets:**  The model zoo is restricted to ViT-S models fine-tuned on CIFAR-100 after pre-training on ImageNet. Expanding to other ViT variants (e.g., ViT-Base, ViT-Large) and datasets would broaden its applicability.
    *   **Performance Optimization:** The authors emphasize diversity over performance. While this is a reasonable design choice, it means that the models may not represent the state-of-the-art in terms of accuracy. This could limit their usefulness for some applications.
     *  The tested weight averaging methods seem to perform fairly poorly, indicating that there is still work needed to determine what techniques can benefit from this model zoo. However, this could be seen as a strength in that it creates a challenging dataset for future work.
*   **Potential Influence:** This model zoo has the potential to become a widely used resource in the vision transformer research community.  It could serve as a benchmark for new methods in model analysis, representation learning, and generative modeling. The two-stage training approach offers a novel perspective on transfer learning.

*   **Justification of the Score:**

    The paper offers a solid contribution by addressing a gap in available model zoos. The structured approach, diversity analysis, and potential applications are all valuable aspects. While the scope is somewhat limited (ViT-S and specific datasets), the impact on the community is likely to be significant, making it a worthwhile contribution.

### XY-Cut++: Advanced Layout Ordering via Hierarchical Mask Mechanism on a Novel Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10258v1)
- **Authors**: Shuai Liu, Youmeng Li, Jizeng Wei
- **Abstract**: Document Reading Order Recovery is a fundamental task in document image understanding, playing a pivotal role in enhancing Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and serving as a critical preprocessing step for large language models (LLMs). Existing methods often struggle with complex layouts(e.g., multi-column newspapers), high-overhead interactions between cross-modal elements (visual regions and textual semantics), and a lack of robust evaluation benchmarks. We introduce XY-Cut++, an advanced layout ordering method that integrates pre-mask processing, multi-granularity segmentation, and cross-modal matching to address these challenges. Our method significantly enhances layout ordering accuracy compared to traditional XY-Cut techniques. Specifically, XY-Cut++ achieves state-of-the-art performance (98.8 BLEU overall) while maintaining simplicity and efficiency. It outperforms existing baselines by up to 24\% and demonstrates consistent accuracy across simple and complex layouts on the newly introduced DocBench-100 dataset. This advancement establishes a reliable foundation for document structure recovery, setting a new standard for layout ordering tasks and facilitating more effective RAG and LLM preprocessing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "XY-Cut++: Advanced Layout Ordering via Hierarchical Mask Mechanism on a Novel Benchmark" introduces an improved layout ordering method for document image understanding.  It builds upon the traditional XY-Cut algorithm, addressing its limitations in handling complex document layouts, inefficient cross-modal alignment, and the lack of robust evaluation benchmarks. The proposed XY-Cut++ incorporates pre-mask processing, multi-granularity segmentation, and cross-modal matching.  The authors also introduce DocBench-100, a new benchmark dataset for evaluating layout ordering techniques. Experimental results demonstrate that XY-Cut++ achieves state-of-the-art performance compared to existing methods, showcasing its accuracy and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents a valuable incremental improvement over existing methods.  While the core idea of XY-Cut is not new, the integration of pre-masking, multi-granularity segmentation, and shallow semantic information represents a significant refinement. The use of shallow semantics rather than deep learning methods is a novel aspect to explore within this field. The DocBench-100 dataset is a key contribution, as it addresses the limitations of existing benchmarks and provides a more comprehensive evaluation resource. The evaluation focuses on performance and efficiency, demonstrating the algorithm's ability to work quickly and accurately, but does not explore resource constraints.

*   **Significance:** The improved layout ordering accuracy has direct implications for enhancing Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems and improving preprocessing for large language models (LLMs).  The DocBench-100 dataset is likely to become a valuable resource for the document image understanding community. The method's efficiency makes it practical for real-world applications, unlike some deep learning-based alternatives with high computational costs. The paper provides detailed ablation studies, offering insights into the contribution of each component. By focusing on shallow semantic information, the method achieves efficiency and allows for deployment with fewer resource constraints, which is valuable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Improved accuracy:** The experimental results clearly demonstrate the superiority of XY-Cut++ over existing methods.
    *   **Efficiency:** The method maintains simplicity and efficiency, making it practical for real-world deployment.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper provides extensive experimental results and ablation studies, offering insights into the method's performance.
    *   **New Benchmark Dataset:** The DocBench-100 dataset fills a crucial gap in the field, providing a more robust and diverse evaluation resource.
    *   **Clarity of Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvement:** The method builds upon existing techniques, and the core idea of XY-Cut is not new.
    *   **Limited scope of semantic information:** The method relies on shallow semantic information, which may not be sufficient for handling more complex document layouts or tasks.
    *   **Lack of comparative analysis**: Comparing the results with other state-of-the-art models that use different benchmarks and techniques can reveal the method's limitations and demonstrate the areas of potential improvement
    *   **Limited consideration for resource constraints**: Even though the method boasts simplicity and efficiency, the paper does not explore the resources used by the method.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The DocBench-100 dataset is likely to become a standard benchmark in the field, fostering more robust and comparable evaluation of layout ordering techniques.
    *   The XY-Cut++ method may inspire further research on incorporating lightweight semantic information into geometric layout analysis methods.
    *   The efficiency of the method makes it a good candidate for integration into practical document processing pipelines.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing an improved layout ordering method and a new benchmark dataset.  The significant improvements in accuracy and efficiency, along with the comprehensive evaluation, demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The emphasis on efficient techniques rather than solely relying on complex deep learning models is a notable aspect. However, the incremental nature of the improvement and the limited scope of semantic information prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. While the authors have presented an innovative and successful system, there is still room for further improvements and optimizations that could further expand the reach of this system. A deeper analysis of the trade-offs between performance and efficiency, with a focus on resource consumption, would also be beneficial. Taking these limitations into account, I would assess the paper as a worthwhile contribution that achieves novel results.

### DiffMOD: Progressive Diffusion Point Denoising for Moving Object Detection in Remote Sensing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10278v1)
- **Authors**: Jinyue Zhang, Xiangrong Zhang, Zhongjian Huang, Tianyang Zhang, Yifei Jiang, Licheng Jiao
- **Abstract**: Moving object detection (MOD) in remote sensing is significantly challenged by low resolution, extremely small object sizes, and complex noise interference. Current deep learning-based MOD methods rely on probability density estimation, which restricts flexible information interaction between objects and across temporal frames. To flexibly capture high-order inter-object and temporal relationships, we propose a point-based MOD in remote sensing. Inspired by diffusion models, the network optimization is formulated as a progressive denoising process that iteratively recovers moving object centers from sparse noisy points. Specifically, we sample scattered features from the backbone outputs as atomic units for subsequent processing, while global feature embeddings are aggregated to compensate for the limited coverage of sparse point features. By modeling spatial relative positions and semantic affinities, Spatial Relation Aggregation Attention is designed to enable high-order interactions among point-level features for enhanced object representation. To enhance temporal consistency, the Temporal Propagation and Global Fusion module is designed, which leverages an implicit memory reasoning mechanism for robust cross-frame feature integration. To align with the progressive denoising process, we propose a progressive MinK optimal transport assignment strategy that establishes specialized learning objectives at each denoising level. Additionally, we introduce a missing loss function to counteract the clustering tendency of denoised points around salient objects. Experiments on the RsData remote sensing MOD dataset show that our MOD method based on scattered point denoising can more effectively explore potential relationships between sparse moving objects and improve the detection capability and temporal consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffMOD, a novel approach for moving object detection (MOD) in remote sensing images. It addresses the challenges of low resolution, small object sizes, and complex noise by framing MOD as a progressive diffusion point denoising process. Instead of directly estimating object probabilities, DiffMOD starts with sparse, noisy points and iteratively refines them to approximate object centers. The method incorporates several key innovations: Spatial Relation Aggregation Attention (SRAA) to capture high-order inter-object relationships, a Temporal Propagation and Global Fusion (TPGF) module for temporal consistency, and a progressive MinK optimal transport assignment strategy, along with a missing loss function, to mitigate clustering artifacts. Experiments on the RsData dataset demonstrate improved performance compared to existing methods, particularly in scenarios where objects are small and the scene is complex.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components:
    *   **Point-based Diffusion for MOD:** Framing MOD as a diffusion-based point denoising task is a creative departure from traditional density estimation approaches. This allows for more flexible information interaction and addresses the limitations of convolutional methods in capturing long-range dependencies.
    *   **Spatial Relation Aggregation Attention (SRAA):** The combination of spatial relationships and semantic affinities within an attention mechanism is a valuable contribution. It allows the network to better reason about the context of each point, improving object discrimination.
    *   **Temporal Propagation and Global Fusion (TPGF):** Leveraging an implicit memory mechanism to maintain temporal consistency is an effective way to handle noise and occlusions in video sequences.
    *   **Progressive MinK OTA & Missing Loss:** Addressing the issue of point clustering in diffusion models with a specialized assignment strategy and a missing loss function is a targeted solution to a specific problem in this context.

*   **Significance:** MOD in remote sensing is a critical task with applications in various fields. The challenges associated with low resolution and small objects make it a particularly difficult problem. DiffMOD's approach tackles these challenges head-on and demonstrates improved performance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured explanation of the proposed method.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of DiffMOD compared to state-of-the-art methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each component of the proposed method.
    *   The method is well-motivated and addresses specific limitations of existing approaches.
    *  The design choice of the various modules clearly target the difficult problems of the remote sensing domain, specifically the temporal reasoning required for noisy satellite data.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the experiments show improved performance on the RsData dataset, it would be beneficial to evaluate the method on other remote sensing datasets to assess its generalizability.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity of DiffMOD compared to other methods. The memory reasoning in the TPGF module is a potential bottle neck, however, this is not critically discussed.
    *   The sensitivity of the method to hyperparameter settings could be further explored.
    *   While the improvements over existing methods are evident, the qualitative results, particularly the visualization of attention maps and denoising process, could be more informative and insightful. The visualization comparisons in Fig 7 are informative, however lack quantitative indication regarding the size and type of targets in consideration.

*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   DiffMOD has the potential to significantly improve MOD performance in remote sensing applications.
    *   The proposed techniques, such as SRAA and TPGF, could be applied to other computer vision tasks.
    *   The point-based diffusion framework could inspire new approaches to object detection and tracking in challenging scenarios.
    *   The detailed ablation study provides valuable guidance for future research in this area.

*   **Overall:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of MOD in remote sensing by introducing a novel and effective point-based diffusion framework. The proposed techniques address the specific challenges of this domain and demonstrate improved performance compared to existing methods. While there are some areas for further investigation, the paper has the potential to inspire new research and applications in remote sensing and computer vision.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The paper offers a valuable contribution with multiple strong novel components to a challenging problem. The adoption of a diffusion model coupled with specifically designed attention mechanism and temporal modeling module makes this paper standout in the field of MOD. However, the potential concerns about generalizability, computational complexity, sensitivity to hyperparameters and limited qualitative results prevent a higher score.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Zero-shot Autonomous Microscopy for Scalable and Intelligent Characterization of 2D Materials
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10281v1)
- **Authors**: Jingyun Yang, Ruoyan Avery Yin, Chi Jiang, Yuepeng Hu, Xiaokai Zhu, Xingjian Hu, Sutharsika Kumar, Xiao Wang, Xiaohua Zhai, Keran Rong, Yunyue Zhu, Tianyi Zhang, Zongyou Yin, Jing Kong, Neil Zhenqiang Gong, Zhichu Ren, Haozhe Wang
- **Abstract**: Characterization of atomic-scale materials traditionally requires human experts with months to years of specialized training. Even for trained human operators, accurate and reliable characterization remains challenging when examining newly discovered materials such as two-dimensional (2D) structures. This bottleneck drives demand for fully autonomous experimentation systems capable of comprehending research objectives without requiring large training datasets. In this work, we present ATOMIC (Autonomous Technology for Optical Microscopy & Intelligent Characterization), an end-to-end framework that integrates foundation models to enable fully autonomous, zero-shot characterization of 2D materials. Our system integrates the vision foundation model (i.e., Segment Anything Model), large language models (i.e., ChatGPT), unsupervised clustering, and topological analysis to automate microscope control, sample scanning, image segmentation, and intelligent analysis through prompt engineering, eliminating the need for additional training. When analyzing typical MoS2 samples, our approach achieves 99.7% segmentation accuracy for single layer identification, which is equivalent to that of human experts. In addition, the integrated model is able to detect grain boundary slits that are challenging to identify with human eyes. Furthermore, the system retains robust accuracy despite variable conditions including defocus, color temperature fluctuations, and exposure variations. It is applicable to a broad spectrum of common 2D materials-including graphene, MoS2, WSe2, SnSe-regardless of whether they were fabricated via chemical vapor deposition or mechanical exfoliation. This work represents the implementation of foundation models to achieve autonomous analysis, establishing a scalable and data-efficient characterization paradigm that fundamentally transforms the approach to nanoscale materials research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ATOMIC (Autonomous Technology for Optical Microscopy & Intelligent Characterization), an end-to-end framework for autonomous characterization of 2D materials using foundation models (FMs). ATOMIC integrates a vision foundation model (Segment Anything Model, SAM), large language models (LLMs, ChatGPT), unsupervised clustering, and topological analysis. The system automates microscope control, sample scanning, image segmentation, and intelligent analysis through prompt engineering, eliminating the need for training data. The paper demonstrates high segmentation accuracy for MoS2 samples, including detection of grain boundary slits, and robustness under varying conditions. The system is applicable to various 2D materials (graphene, MoS2, WSe2, SnSe) fabricated by different methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key strength of this paper is the integration of foundation models into a workflow for materials characterization. While machine learning has been applied to materials science previously, the *zero-shot* capability afforded by foundation models significantly reduces the need for labeled training data, a major bottleneck in materials discovery.  The use of LLMs to guide microscopy control and select clustering parameters is also a novel aspect. The topological correction to SAM masks to improve clustering accuracy is a valuable addition, but not groundbreaking on its own.

* **Significance:** The potential impact on materials research is substantial. Autonomous characterization accelerates the discovery and analysis of new materials. The data efficiency of the ATOMIC framework is particularly important for analyzing newly discovered materials where large, high-quality training datasets are unavailable. Furthermore, the system's ability to identify features like grain boundary slits, often missed by human eyes, could have significant implications for materials processing and device fabrication. The system's demonstrated robustness is another significant point, implying that the method can be more easily applied and maintained across diverse experimental conditions.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Zero-Shot Capability:** Reduced reliance on labeled data is a major advantage.
    *   **End-to-End Automation:** The system automates the entire workflow, from microscope control to data analysis.
    *   **Detection of Subtle Features:** Ability to identify grain boundary slits beyond human perception.
    *   **Robustness:** Demonstrated resilience to variable imaging conditions.
    *   **Generality:** Applicable to diverse 2D materials and fabrication methods.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The framework's performance depends on the capabilities of underlying foundation models (SAM, ChatGPT). Future improvements or limitations in these models directly affect ATOMIC's performance.  The paper doesn't address scenarios where the FMs fail, and how the system would respond.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:**  While MoS2 is a common material, the evaluation could benefit from a more extensive study across a wider range of materials and applications. The supplementary materials detail some of these other applications, but further detail would be helpful.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While not explicitly discussed, it's implied that running SAM and LLMs could lead to potentially substantial computational resource demands.

* **Potential Influence:** ATOMIC establishes a compelling paradigm for AI-driven materials characterization. It is likely to encourage further research into the application of foundation models in materials science and other scientific disciplines. The approach could be adapted to other microscopy techniques (e.g., AFM, TEM) or other characterization methods (e.g., spectroscopy).

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a significant advancement by demonstrating the feasibility of autonomous materials characterization using foundation models. The zero-shot learning capability, combined with the end-to-end automation, represents a major step towards accelerated materials discovery. While the system relies on existing foundation models and would benefit from a broader evaluation, the demonstrated capabilities and potential impact on the field warrant a high score.
