### DUE: A Deep Learning Framework and Library for Modeling Unknown Equations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10373v1)
- **Authors**: Junfeng Chen, Kailiang Wu, Dongbin Xiu
- **Abstract**: Equations, particularly differential equations, are fundamental for understanding natural phenomena and predicting complex dynamics across various scientific and engineering disciplines. However, the governing equations for many complex systems remain unknown due to intricate underlying mechanisms. Recent advancements in machine learning and data science offer a new paradigm for modeling unknown equations from measurement or simulation data. This paradigm shift, known as data-driven discovery or modeling, stands at the forefront of AI for science, with significant progress made in recent years. In this paper, we introduce a systematic framework for data-driven modeling of unknown equations using deep learning. This versatile framework is capable of learning unknown ODEs, PDEs, DAEs, IDEs, SDEs, reduced or partially observed systems, and non-autonomous differential equations. Based on this framework, we have developed Deep Unknown Equations (DUE), an open-source software package designed to facilitate the data-driven modeling of unknown equations using modern deep learning techniques. DUE serves as an educational tool for classroom instruction, enabling students and newcomers to gain hands-on experience with differential equations, data-driven modeling, and contemporary deep learning approaches such as FNN, ResNet, generalized ResNet, operator semigroup networks (OSG-Net), and Transformers. Additionally, DUE is a versatile and accessible toolkit for researchers across various scientific and engineering fields. It is applicable not only for learning unknown equations from data but also for surrogate modeling of known, yet complex, equations that are costly to solve using traditional numerical methods. We provide detailed descriptions of DUE and demonstrate its capabilities through diverse examples, which serve as templates that can be easily adapted for other applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DUE (Deep Unknown Equations), an open-source Python framework and library designed to simplify the data-driven modeling of unknown differential equations using deep learning.  DUE aims to provide a versatile tool for both educational purposes (allowing students to experiment with differential equations and deep learning) and research (providing a toolkit for scientists and engineers).  It supports various types of equations (ODEs, PDEs, DAEs, IDEs, SDEs) and incorporates several modern deep learning architectures (FNNs, ResNets, gResNets, OSG-Nets, Transformers). The paper outlines the framework's structure (datasets, networks, models modules), discusses key techniques (multi-step loss, semigroup-informed loss, dual-network architectures), and demonstrates its capabilities through several examples, including damped pendulum, coupled oscillators, Lorenz system, Robertson chemical reaction, viscous Burgers' equation, and Navier-Stokes equations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the creation and packaging of a comprehensive, accessible, and versatile framework for data-driven modeling of unknown equations. While the individual deep learning architectures and some of the training techniques have been previously published, DUE provides a unified platform and streamlined workflow for their application. The dual-OSG-Net architecture is introduced as a novel contribution for handling multiscale dynamics.
* **Significance:** The significance stems from several aspects:
    *   **Accessibility:** DUE lowers the barrier to entry for researchers and students wanting to explore data-driven equation discovery. The ease of use (as few as ten lines of code) will likely encourage wider adoption.
    *   **Versatility:** The framework's ability to handle a wide range of equation types and deep learning architectures makes it adaptable to various scientific and engineering problems.
    *   **Educational Value:** It provides a practical learning tool for understanding differential equations, deep learning, and their integration.
    *   **Surrogate Modeling:** The potential for using DUE as a surrogate model for computationally expensive equations is also a valuable contribution.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** DUE provides a well-structured and organized framework covering data processing, network architectures, and model training.
    *   **Variety of Examples:** The paper includes a good selection of examples demonstrating the framework's capabilities across different types of equations and applications.
    *   **Clear Explanations:** The descriptions of the different modules, architectures, and techniques are generally clear and well-explained.
    *   **Open-Source:** The open-source nature of the project promotes collaboration, extension, and wider adoption.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty in Methods:** While the framework itself is novel, some of the individual techniques (e.g., multi-step loss, semigroup-informed loss, dual-OSG-Net) are built upon existing work. The reported superiority of dual-OSG-Net is only demonstrated using one example, which calls into question the general applicability of this method.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Performance:** While examples are provided, a more thorough and systematic analysis of the performance of DUE compared to other existing tools or methods would strengthen the paper.  Metrics such as computational cost, robustness to noise, and generalization ability could be analyzed more deeply. A more direct comparison to similar software packages such as DeepXDE and NeuralUQ would be valuable.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Guarantees:** As with many deep learning methods, DUE lacks theoretical guarantees regarding convergence or accuracy.
    *   **Dependency on Data Quality:** The success of DUE heavily depends on the quality and quantity of the training data.  The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of data requirements and pre-processing strategies.

* **Potential Impact:** DUE has the potential to significantly impact the field by democratizing access to data-driven equation discovery and accelerating research in various scientific and engineering disciplines. Its educational value is also noteworthy.

**Justification for the Score:**

While DUE is a valuable contribution to the field, its novelty is primarily in the integration and accessibility of existing methods rather than in groundbreaking new theoretical developments. The framework lowers the barrier to entry and fosters wider experimentation, but the impact will depend on its adoption and the insights it enables in real-world applications. While there are areas for further improvement, the comprehensiveness and usability are compelling.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can LLMs Assist Expert Elicitation for Probabilistic Causal Modeling?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10397v1)
- **Authors**: Olha Shaposhnyk, Daria Zahorska, Svetlana Yanushkevich
- **Abstract**: Objective: This study investigates the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) as an alternative to human expert elicitation for extracting structured causal knowledge and facilitating causal modeling in biometric and healthcare applications. Material and Methods: LLM-generated causal structures, specifically Bayesian networks (BNs), were benchmarked against traditional statistical methods (e.g., Bayesian Information Criterion) using healthcare datasets. Validation techniques included structural equation modeling (SEM) to verifying relationships, and measures such as entropy, predictive accuracy, and robustness to compare network structures. Results and Discussion: LLM-generated BNs demonstrated lower entropy than expert-elicited and statistically generated BNs, suggesting higher confidence and precision in predictions. However, limitations such as contextual constraints, hallucinated dependencies, and potential biases inherited from training data require further investigation. Conclusion: LLMs represent a novel frontier in expert elicitation for probabilistic causal modeling, promising to improve transparency and reduce uncertainty in the decision-making using such models.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) as a tool to facilitate expert elicitation in probabilistic causal modeling, specifically for constructing Bayesian Networks (BNs). The authors investigate if LLMs can serve as an alternative to traditional expert elicitation methods, which are often time-consuming and subjective. The study applies this approach to a 'Sleep, Health, and Lifestyle' dataset and compares the LLM-generated BNs with those created using established statistical methods (Bayesian Information Criterion - BIC, PC algorithms) and human experts. They evaluate the generated BNs using structural equation modeling (SEM), entropy, predictive accuracy, and robustness. The results indicate that LLM-generated BNs exhibit lower entropy, suggesting higher confidence and precision in predictions, but acknowledge limitations like contextual constraints, hallucinated dependencies, and potential biases. The authors conclude that LLMs hold promise for improving transparency and reducing uncertainty in decision-making involving probabilistic causal models.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using LLMs as a replacement *or* supplement to expert elicitation in causal modeling is relatively novel. While previous studies (cited in the paper) have explored LLMs for causal discovery and building prior distributions, the direct application and comparison against human experts and statistical methods in the specific context of biometric/healthcare data appears to be a valuable contribution. There have been papers focusing more on utilizing LLMs for causal discovery from text or observational data. However, in this work, the goal is to use LLMs to elicit causal knowledge.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to address the bottlenecks and limitations of traditional expert elicitation. If LLMs can effectively extract structured causal knowledge, it could lead to more efficient and scalable causal modeling processes. The application to healthcare is particularly relevant, given the complexity of the domain and the need for interpretable models. The lowered entropy shown for LLM-generated BNs is encouraging. However, the study also identifies important limitations that need to be addressed for practical deployment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and research question.
    *   Well-defined methodology involving a comparison of different BN generation approaches.
    *   Use of relevant evaluation metrics (entropy, SEM).
    *   Identification of important limitations (hallucinations, biases, contextual constraints).
    *   The paper tackles a real-world problem with a potentially disruptive solution.
    *   The authors use the dual LLM prompting to attempt to overcome possible hallucinations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited scope of the case study:**  The study is based on a single dataset. It's unclear how well the findings generalize to other domains or datasets with different characteristics.
    *   **Depth of validation:** While the paper uses multiple validation techniques, the specific details of how these techniques were applied and the interpretation of the results could be further elaborated. For instance, there is little discussion about how to deal with the inherent biases that come with data, and how this impacts the generated LLM causal graph. Similarly, while the hallucinations that occur in LLMs are mentioned, there isn't much effort spent trying to limit it in this study.
    *   **Hallucinations and Bias:** The paper acknowledges the issue of LLM hallucinations and potential biases, but the mitigation strategies seem relatively limited.  More rigorous methods for addressing these challenges are needed. The authors are overly reliant on two LLMs to double check each other.
    *   **Clarity of Prompts:** While the prompt engineering methodology is briefly mentioned, there are limited examples of the actual prompts used. This makes it difficult to replicate the study and assess the quality of the LLM's outputs.
    *   **Comparison to Human Experts:**  The level of expertise of the human experts used for comparison isn't clear. Comparing to "average" expert knowledge may not be as informative as comparing to highly specialized experts.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the identified limitations can be overcome, the paper could influence the way causal models are built in various domains, especially those with complex data and a need for expert knowledge. This could lead to more transparent, efficient, and scalable decision-making processes. It may also spur more research in the area of LLM-augmented expert systems.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a promising avenue for leveraging LLMs in causal modeling, but it requires further research and development to address the identified limitations. The significance of this paper is the first step into the area of LLM expert elicitation.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**
The paper showcases some novelty and potential in its use of LLMs for causal modeling expert elicitation. However, it is limited by the narrow dataset, relatively shallow validation, and a need for more robust mitigation strategies for LLM-specific challenges (hallucinations, biases). Moreover, a clearer specification of the limitations of expert domain-knowledge and statistical approaches is needed. While the initial results are encouraging, more rigorous experimentation and a broader scope of evaluation are necessary to justify a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Performance of Large Language Models in Supporting Medical Diagnosis and Treatment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10405v1)
- **Authors**: Diogo Sousa, Guilherme Barbosa, Catarina Rocha, Dulce Oliveira
- **Abstract**: The integration of Large Language Models (LLMs) into healthcare holds significant potential to enhance diagnostic accuracy and support medical treatment planning. These AI-driven systems can analyze vast datasets, assisting clinicians in identifying diseases, recommending treatments, and predicting patient outcomes. This study evaluates the performance of a range of contemporary LLMs, including both open-source and closed-source models, on the 2024 Portuguese National Exam for medical specialty access (PNA), a standardized medical knowledge assessment. Our results highlight considerable variation in accuracy and cost-effectiveness, with several models demonstrating performance exceeding human benchmarks for medical students on this specific task. We identify leading models based on a combined score of accuracy and cost, discuss the implications of reasoning methodologies like Chain-of-Thought, and underscore the potential for LLMs to function as valuable complementary tools aiding medical professionals in complex clinical decision-making.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the performance of a diverse range of Large Language Models (LLMs) on a standardized medical knowledge assessment: the 2024 Portuguese National Exam for medical specialty access (PNA). The authors evaluate both open-source and closed-source models, focusing on accuracy and cost-effectiveness. They introduce a custom scoring metric that balances these factors, also considering the risk of data contamination (given knowledge cutoff dates). The results highlight the variation in LLM performance, with several models surpassing human student benchmarks. The paper discusses the implications of different reasoning methodologies (like Chain-of-Thought), the impact of knowledge cutoff dates, and ethical considerations for deploying LLMs in healthcare. The study emphasizes LLMs as assistive tools rather than replacements for clinicians, calling for continued research and rigorous validation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its evaluation of *current* (2024-2025 timeframe) LLMs on a *specific* standardized medical exam (PNA) in *Portuguese*. While previous works have explored LLMs on medical licensing exams, this paper provides an updated assessment of the rapidly evolving landscape. The custom scoring metric, combining accuracy, cost, and contamination risk, offers a useful, but not revolutionary, approach to comparing models. The live, blinded benchmark suggestion is reasonable but not particularly innovative, though it would strengthen validation.

*   **Significance:** The study's significance stems from its practical implications for clinical decision support. By evaluating models based on cost-effectiveness, the paper provides insights relevant to real-world deployment viability. The discussion of potential benefits (rapid information retrieval, suggesting diagnoses) and risks (hallucinations, biases) is valuable for healthcare professionals considering integrating LLMs into their workflows. The mapping of LLM use cases to the EU AI Act is a helpful contribution to the regulatory discussion.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The inclusion of both open-source and closed-source models provides a comprehensive performance landscape.
    *   The focus on cost-effectiveness is critical for healthcare applications, making the evaluation practical.
    *   The contamination risk assessment is a thoughtful consideration, given the timing of the PNA exam and LLM knowledge cutoffs.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly articulates the findings, limitations, and future directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation is limited to a single, multiple-choice exam. This may not fully capture the complexities of real-time clinical decision-making.
    *   The focus on a Portuguese exam limits generalizability. While valuable for the specific context, its relevance to other languages and healthcare systems is uncertain.
    *   The performance metric, while useful, relies on approximate cost estimations, which are subject to change and may not reflect actual real-world pricing.
    *   The study lacks a systematic qualitative error analysis. Understanding the *types* of errors made by the LLMs (e.g., reasoning errors vs. knowledge gaps) would provide deeper insights.
    *   The claim of one LLM slightly surpassing maximum student score is not particularly meaningful since the scoring metric penalizes for cost and potential contamination.
    *   It's essentially a performance benchmark on a specific task, a valuable but not fundamentally transformative exercise.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence healthcare professionals and AI developers interested in integrating LLMs into clinical practice. The practical insights regarding cost-effectiveness and reasoning methodologies can inform decision-making. The ethical and regulatory considerations will contribute to ongoing discussions about responsible LLM deployment in healthcare. Its impact is limited by its specific context (Portuguese exam) and the rapidly evolving nature of the field.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper is well-executed and addresses a relevant topic, its novelty and significance are limited. Existing literature has already explored LLMs in medical contexts. This paper offers a valuable *update* and a *practical* evaluation, but doesn't introduce fundamentally new concepts or methodologies. Its contributions are incremental rather than groundbreaking.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid, well-structured evaluation of current LLMs on a specific medical exam, focusing on cost-effectiveness. It provides valuable insights for practical deployment but its limited scope, lack of qualitative error analysis, and incremental contributions restrict its overall significance and novelty. It represents a competent piece of applied research within a rapidly evolving field, making it worthy of a score reflecting a solid contribution without being a transformative one.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLM-SRBench: A New Benchmark for Scientific Equation Discovery with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10415v1)
- **Authors**: Parshin Shojaee, Ngoc-Hieu Nguyen, Kazem Meidani, Amir Barati Farimani, Khoa D Doan, Chandan K Reddy
- **Abstract**: Scientific equation discovery is a fundamental task in the history of scientific progress, enabling the derivation of laws governing natural phenomena. Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have gained interest for this task due to their potential to leverage embedded scientific knowledge for hypothesis generation. However, evaluating the true discovery capabilities of these methods remains challenging, as existing benchmarks often rely on common equations that are susceptible to memorization by LLMs, leading to inflated performance metrics that do not reflect discovery. In this paper, we introduce LLM-SRBench, a comprehensive benchmark with 239 challenging problems across four scientific domains specifically designed to evaluate LLM-based scientific equation discovery methods while preventing trivial memorization. Our benchmark comprises two main categories: LSR-Transform, which transforms common physical models into less common mathematical representations to test reasoning beyond memorized forms, and LSR-Synth, which introduces synthetic, discovery-driven problems requiring data-driven reasoning. Through extensive evaluation of several state-of-the-art methods, using both open and closed LLMs, we find that the best-performing system so far achieves only 31.5% symbolic accuracy. These findings highlight the challenges of scientific equation discovery, positioning LLM-SRBench as a valuable resource for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-SRBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in scientific equation discovery. Addressing the limitations of existing benchmarks that are prone to memorization, LLM-SRBench features 239 challenging problems across four scientific domains. The benchmark has two categories: LSR-Transform, which transforms common physical models into less common mathematical representations, and LSR-Synth, which introduces synthetic, discovery-driven problems. Experimental results, using both open and closed LLMs, revealed that the best-performing system achieves only 31.5% symbolic accuracy, highlighting the difficulty of the task and emphasizing LLM-SRBench's value as a resource for future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in recognizing the shortcomings of existing benchmarks and providing a solution targeted explicitly at evaluating LLMs for *true* scientific discovery, not just recitation. Transforming existing Feynman equations into unfamiliar forms (LSR-Transform) is a clever strategy to mitigate memorization. The inclusion of synthetic, discovery-driven problems (LSR-Synth) to force data-driven reasoning is also a significant contribution. While custom-crafted problems have been used before, the scale and diversity of LLM-SRBench are substantial.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the fact that it provides a much-needed tool for objectively assessing LLMs in a domain where they are often overhyped. By highlighting the limitations of current LLM-based approaches, it grounds the discussion in reality and helps to direct future research towards more robust and genuinely capable methods. The benchmark's comprehensiveness, covering several scientific domains and different aspects of equation discovery, increases its potential impact. The provided results act as clear baselines for the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The design of the benchmark to avoid memorization is well thought out and justified.
    *   The benchmark is comprehensive, covering a reasonable number of problems across different scientific domains.
    *   The paper provides baseline results using several state-of-the-art methods, making it easy to compare future approaches.
    *   The analysis of results gives a nuanced picture of the strengths and weaknesses of different LLMs and discovery methods.
    * The dataset and source code are promised to be open-sourced, which increases its utility to the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While diverse, the four scientific domains included are still limited compared to the vast expanse of scientific knowledge. Expanding the benchmark to include more domains would be beneficial.
    * The LLM-based evaluation of symbolic accuracy is potentially subjective, despite the high inter-annotator agreement. While GPT-4 is used as an evaluator, the prompt engineering and reliance on language models for a task of mathematical equivalence should be approached with caution and validated thoroughly.
    *  The paper could benefit from a deeper error analysis, investigating the kinds of errors LLMs make and why. Identifying specific patterns or common failure modes would provide valuable insights for improving LLM-based scientific discovery methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLMs in scientific equation discovery. It will help to focus research efforts on developing methods that go beyond memorization and are capable of genuine scientific reasoning. The benchmark will also be valuable for assessing the impact of future advances in LLMs and for identifying the types of scientific problems where LLMs can be most effectively applied.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper is a strong contribution, it is not without limitations. The potential subjectivity of the LLM-based symbolic equivalence metric, and the limited scope of the scientific domains it encompasses, slightly lower the score. That said, the value in creating a rigorous benchmark for a highly relevant task is significant. The design and methodology are well thought out and the paper is clearly written.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### CliniChat: A Multi-Source Knowledge-Driven Framework for Clinical Interview Dialogue Reconstruction and Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10418v1)
- **Authors**: Jing Chen, Zhihua Wei, Wei Zhang, Yingying Hu, Qiong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) hold great promise for assisting clinical interviews due to their fluent interactive capabilities and extensive medical knowledge. However, the lack of high-quality interview dialogue data and widely accepted evaluation methods has significantly impeded this process. So we propose CliniChat, a framework that integrates multi-source knowledge to enable LLMs to simulate real-world clinical interviews. It consists of two modules: Clini-Recon and Clini-Eval, each responsible for reconstructing and evaluating interview dialogues, respectively. By incorporating three sources of knowledge, Clini-Recon transforms clinical notes into systematic, professional, and empathetic interview dialogues. Clini-Eval combines a comprehensive evaluation metric system with a two-phase automatic evaluation approach, enabling LLMs to assess interview performance like experts. We contribute MedQA-Dialog, a high-quality synthetic interview dialogue dataset, and CliniChatGLM, a model specialized for clinical interviews. Experimental results demonstrate that CliniChatGLM's interview capabilities undergo a comprehensive upgrade, particularly in history-taking, achieving state-of-the-art performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CliniChat: A Multi-Source Knowledge-Driven Framework for Clinical Interview Dialogue Reconstruction and Evaluation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CliniChat, a framework designed to improve the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to conduct simulated clinical interviews. Recognizing the lack of high-quality interview dialogue data and standardized evaluation methods, CliniChat tackles the problem through two main modules:

*   **Clini-Recon:** This module reconstructs clinical interview dialogues from clinical notes using a multi-source knowledge approach. It incorporates patient interview guidelines, LLM knowledge, and input from physicians to generate dialogues that are systematic, professional, and empathetic. It essentially fills in the gaps present in static clinical notes to create more realistic interactions.
*   **Clini-Eval:** This module evaluates the interview performance of LLMs using a comprehensive metric system and a two-phase automatic evaluation approach called Demo2Eval. The system involves generating a demonstration of an ideal interview, and then comparing the target model output to this demonstration.

The authors create MedQA-Dialog, a high-quality synthetic interview dialogue dataset derived from MedQA-USMLE case study questions. They then fine-tune ChatGLM2-6B to create CliniChatGLM, a model specialized for clinical interviews. Experimental results suggest CliniChatGLM achieves state-of-the-art performance, particularly in history taking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to addressing the scarcity of clinical interview dialogue data. The multi-source knowledge integration in Clini-Recon is a significant improvement over simpler role-play prompting techniques. The Demo2Eval evaluation method, while inspired by clinical teaching, offers a structured and automated approach to assessing interview performance, which is also novel. Creating a high quality interview dataset, MedQA-Dialog, is also a useful contribution.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing Data Scarcity:**  The major contribution is addressing the bottleneck of limited interview dialogue data, which is a significant hurdle in the development of LLM-assisted clinical interview systems. The synthetic data generation approach is well-motivated.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  Clini-Eval provides a much more comprehensive and well-defined evaluation framework than previously used. The attention to the nuances of clinical interviews, as reflected in the evaluation metrics, is valuable.
*   **Improved Interview Capabilities:** The experimental results showing improved performance in history-taking is a notable achievement, suggesting that CliniChat can effectively transform clinical notes into realistic interview scenarios.
*   **Practical Relevance:** This research has the potential to contribute to more effective LLM-based tools that can assist physicians in clinical interviews, ultimately leading to better patient care.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Defined Framework:** The paper clearly outlines the architecture of CliniChat and the functionalities of its two modules.
*   **Multi-Source Knowledge Integration:** The paper convincingly demonstrates how incorporating various sources of knowledge improves the quality of the generated dialogues.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation Methodology:** Clini-Eval offers a thorough and structured way to evaluate LLM-based interview systems.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The paper provides compelling evidence that CliniChatGLM outperforms existing approaches in specific areas.
*   **Contribution to the Research Community:** The release of the MedQA-Dialog dataset and code will be valuable to the community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Synthetic Data:** While addressing a data scarcity issue, the framework fundamentally depends on synthetic data. The extent to which the learned skills generalize to real-world interview data remains an open question.
*   **Evaluation Bias:** Evaluation metrics, while comprehensive, can be prone to biases. The evaluation methodology is also constrained to a single LLM generating the demonstration and evaluation and is dependent on the quality of the GPT-4 model. Incorporating human expert feedback would improve the validity of the results.
*   **Limited Scope:** The current focus is on initial patient consultations. The framework might need to be adapted to handle more complex scenarios, such as follow-up appointments or specialized consultations.
*   **Complexity of implementation:** Replicating the results will require significant effort in prompt engineering and potentially substantial cloud compute.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the development of LLM-assisted clinical interview systems. The framework can be used to improve the data used to train models and to rigorously evaluate their performance.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**. This is a strong score reflecting the paper's sound methodology, novel contributions, and potential impact on the field. While the limitations related to synthetic data, potential evaluation bias, and scope are valid concerns, the paper represents a significant step forward in addressing the challenges of using LLMs for clinical interviews. The comprehensive framework, especially the evaluation system, will likely be adopted by other researchers, and the released resources will facilitate further progress. The paper provides a concrete and replicable way to move closer to AI-assisted systems which can meaningfully improve interactions between patients and doctors.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unchecked and Overlooked: Addressing the Checkbox Blind Spot in Large Language Models with CheckboxQA
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10419v1)
- **Authors**: Michał Turski, Mateusz Chiliński, Łukasz Borchmann
- **Abstract**: Checkboxes are critical in real-world document processing where the presence or absence of ticks directly informs data extraction and decision-making processes. Yet, despite the strong performance of Large Vision and Language Models across a wide range of tasks, they struggle with interpreting checkable content. This challenge becomes particularly pressing in industries where a single overlooked checkbox may lead to costly regulatory or contractual oversights. To address this gap, we introduce the CheckboxQA dataset, a targeted resource designed to evaluate and improve model performance on checkbox-related tasks. It reveals the limitations of current models and serves as a valuable tool for advancing document comprehension systems, with significant implications for applications in sectors such as legal tech and finance. The dataset is publicly available at: https://github.com/Snowflake-Labs/CheckboxQA
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CheckboxQA, a new dataset designed to evaluate and improve the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to interpret checkboxes in visually rich documents.  The authors argue that while LLMs have demonstrated strong performance across various tasks, they often struggle with checkbox interpretation, a crucial aspect of real-world document processing. The CheckboxQA dataset consists of diverse documents annotated with question-answer pairs that require accurate checkbox interpretation. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art commercial and open-source LLMs on the dataset, revealing their limitations. They also provide a qualitative analysis of common failure modes, such as misaligned checkbox and text context, defaulting to textual clues instead of checkbox states, and confusing question text as the answer. The paper concludes by highlighting the importance of accurately interpreting checkboxes and the potential for CheckboxQA to catalyze research in this area.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the identification of a specific blind spot within LLMs applied to document understanding: checkbox interpretation. While Document VQA is an existing area, focusing specifically on checkboxes is a useful contribution. The CheckboxQA dataset itself represents a novel resource designed to address this blind spot. The types of questions asked are also aligned with common use cases.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical importance of checkboxes in real-world document processing, especially in legal and financial contexts. The paper demonstrates that even state-of-the-art LLMs struggle with this seemingly simple task, highlighting a gap in current model capabilities. Correctly interpreting checkboxes can be critical in avoiding costly errors, so the creation of a dedicated evaluation set is welcome. The analysis provided of failure modes for the tested models is also of great value to the wider research community, and gives a guide for future model designers to improve on.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear identification of a practical problem.
    *   Creation of a targeted dataset to address the problem.
    *   Evaluation of a range of LLMs, including both commercial and open-source models.
    *   Qualitative analysis of failure modes, providing insights for future research.
    *   The dataset and analysis offer a valuable tool for advancing document comprehension systems.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset is relatively small with only 88 documents, this limits the ability to train models directly on the data (it must be used for evaluation)
    *   The dataset is limited to English documents, potentially restricting its applicability to other languages and character sets.
    *   The document collection may not be fully representative of all real-world forms, particularly in specialized domains.
    *   The metrics used may not fully capture the nuances of checkbox interpretation, as a single "partially correct" score can be misleading.

*   **Overall Impact:** The paper addresses a concrete and practically important problem, and provides useful tools for addressing it. The dataset and benchmark should encourage further research into improving checkbox interpretation in LLMs. The qualitative analysis has important implications for training data, model architectures, and the need for improved visual reasoning.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of 7.5. The paper presents a well-defined problem and a targeted dataset. Its strengths lie in its clear focus and practical relevance. The weakness of the limited dataset is acknowledged and will hopefully be expanded upon in the future, but overall the paper presents a valuable resource for advancing research in this domain.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can We Edit LLMs for Long-Tail Biomedical Knowledge?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10421v1)
- **Authors**: Xinhao Yi, Jake Lever, Kevin Bryson, Zaiqiao Meng
- **Abstract**: Knowledge editing has emerged as an effective approach for updating large language models (LLMs) by modifying their internal knowledge. However, their application to the biomedical domain faces unique challenges due to the long-tailed distribution of biomedical knowledge, where rare and infrequent information is prevalent. In this paper, we conduct the first comprehensive study to investigate the effectiveness of knowledge editing methods for editing long-tail biomedical knowledge. Our results indicate that, while existing editing methods can enhance LLMs' performance on long-tail biomedical knowledge, their performance on long-tail knowledge remains inferior to that on high-frequency popular knowledge, even after editing. Our further analysis reveals that long-tail biomedical knowledge contains a significant amount of one-to-many knowledge, where one subject and relation link to multiple objects. This high prevalence of one-to-many knowledge limits the effectiveness of knowledge editing in improving LLMs' understanding of long-tail biomedical knowledge, highlighting the need for tailored strategies to bridge this performance gap.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Can We Edit LLMs for Long-Tail Biomedical Knowledge?":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of knowledge editing techniques for long-tail biomedical knowledge in Large Language Models (LLMs).  It finds that while knowledge editing can improve LLMs' performance on this type of knowledge, the performance remains inferior to that achieved on high-frequency (popular) biomedical knowledge.  The paper identifies the prevalence of "one-to-many" relationships in long-tail biomedical knowledge as a key factor limiting the effectiveness of existing knowledge editing methods. Essentially, the study shows that current editing methods struggle to inject and generalize rare but vital biomedical facts, particularly when those facts involve a single concept being linked to multiple related concepts.  The work highlights the need for tailored strategies to specifically address the characteristics of long-tail biomedical knowledge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its focus on the application of knowledge editing techniques to the *biomedical domain*, specifically addressing the challenges posed by the long-tailed distribution of biomedical knowledge. While knowledge editing itself is not a new area, its systematic evaluation within this specific context, including the identification of one-to-many relations as a contributing factor, represents a novel perspective. Also, it's the first comprehensive study focusing on the effectiveness of knowledge editing for *long-tail* biomedical knowledge, and this is significant. However, the editing methods employed are existing ones. The novelty resides more in the *problem framed and analyzed* and the insights derived, rather than in novel algorithmic contributions.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from the increasing use of LLMs in healthcare.  If LLMs are to be reliably deployed in clinical practice, they must be able to accurately handle rare but critical biomedical knowledge. The paper identifies a significant gap in the ability of current knowledge editing methods to address this need.  Furthermore, the paper provides valuable insights into the limitations of existing approaches when dealing with one-to-many relationships, which can inform the development of more effective and tailored knowledge editing strategies.  The work's conclusions have important implications for the design and deployment of LLMs in biomedical applications. The analysis on one-to-many relationship is particularly significant as it reveals a concrete challenge that the community can address.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly articulates the research question, methodology, and findings.
    *   The experimental setup is relatively rigorous, using multiple LLMs and knowledge editing methods.
    *   The analysis is thorough, going beyond simply reporting performance metrics to investigate the underlying reasons for the observed limitations.
    *   The identification of one-to-many relationships as a contributing factor is a valuable insight.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper primarily relies on existing knowledge editing methods. While evaluating these methods is valuable, developing novel methods specifically designed for long-tail biomedical knowledge would further enhance the work's contribution.
    *   The reliance on document-level co-occurrence as a proxy for knowledge popularity may miss finer-grained relationships within sentences.
    *   The analysis is limited to a single knowledge graph (SNOMED CT) and a specific type of biomedical literature (PubMed).

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper can serve as a benchmark study for evaluating future knowledge editing methods designed for long-tail biomedical knowledge. It can stimulate research into techniques that better handle one-to-many relationships and other specific challenges of this domain.  The insights provided will also be valuable for practitioners who are developing and deploying LLMs in healthcare settings.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by systematically evaluating knowledge editing for long-tail biomedical knowledge, uncovering its limitations, and providing insights into the challenges arising from one-to-many relationships. This is a *novel and significant contribution within the field of Biomedical NLP and Knowledge Editing*. While the paper does not propose novel editing methods, it provides a clear problem framing and an analysis of existing methods that lays the groundwork for future research. The emphasis on a concrete challenge (one-to-many relationships) increases its impact. *Its contribution is further bolstered by the increasing reliance on LLMs in biomedicine*. A higher score would be warranted if it presented new editing methods tailored to the biomedical domain.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM Can be a Dangerous Persuader: Empirical Study of Persuasion Safety in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10430v1)
- **Authors**: Minqian Liu, Zhiyang Xu, Xinyi Zhang, Heajun An, Sarvech Qadir, Qi Zhang, Pamela J. Wisniewski, Jin-Hee Cho, Sang Won Lee, Ruoxi Jia, Lifu Huang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have enabled them to approach human-level persuasion capabilities. However, such potential also raises concerns about the safety risks of LLM-driven persuasion, particularly their potential for unethical influence through manipulation, deception, exploitation of vulnerabilities, and many other harmful tactics. In this work, we present a systematic investigation of LLM persuasion safety through two critical aspects: (1) whether LLMs appropriately reject unethical persuasion tasks and avoid unethical strategies during execution, including cases where the initial persuasion goal appears ethically neutral, and (2) how influencing factors like personality traits and external pressures affect their behavior. To this end, we introduce PersuSafety, the first comprehensive framework for the assessment of persuasion safety which consists of three stages, i.e., persuasion scene creation, persuasive conversation simulation, and persuasion safety assessment. PersuSafety covers 6 diverse unethical persuasion topics and 15 common unethical strategies. Through extensive experiments across 8 widely used LLMs, we observe significant safety concerns in most LLMs, including failing to identify harmful persuasion tasks and leveraging various unethical persuasion strategies. Our study calls for more attention to improve safety alignment in progressive and goal-driven conversations such as persuasion.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces PERSUSAFETY, a comprehensive framework to assess the persuasion safety of Large Language Models (LLMs) in dynamic, goal-driven conversations. PERSUSAFETY consists of three stages: persuasion task generation (including ethical and unethical scenarios), persuasive conversation simulation, and persuasion safety assessment. The paper investigates if LLMs can appropriately reject unethical persuasion tasks, avoid unethical strategies, and how factors like personality traits and external pressures influence their behavior. Through experiments across 8 LLMs, the study reveals significant safety concerns, including failures to identify harmful tasks and the use of unethical persuasion strategies. The research shows that even the safest models in refusal can still exhibit high unethical strategy usage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic investigation of LLM persuasion safety using a comprehensive framework, PERSUSAFETY. While prior work has explored LLM safety concerning biases, misinformation, and toxicity, this study uniquely focuses on persuasion-specific risks, particularly in multi-turn dialogues, which raises ethical concerns. Creating the detailed PERSUSAFETY framework, defining personality profiles, and carefully curating ethical and unethical persuasion tasks are valuable contributions.
*   **Significance:** The findings highlight concerning safety issues in widely used LLMs, demonstrating their propensity to exploit vulnerabilities and employ unethical strategies, even in ethically neutral scenarios. The paper underscores the urgent need for improved safety alignment techniques that go beyond simply refusing harmful tasks. It's significant that the study reveals a mismatch between a model's ability to refuse a task and its ethical behavior during task execution, signaling limitations in current safety alignment methods. The finding that external pressures influence LLMs' ethical boundaries is also noteworthy.
*   **Strengths:** The paper's strengths include:
    *   A well-defined and comprehensive framework (PERSUSAFETY).
    *   Thorough experiments across a variety of LLMs.
    *   Detailed analysis of factors influencing persuasion safety.
    *   Clear identification of key concerns about LLM persuasion.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are conducted with LLM-simulated users, which could limit the real-world applicability of the findings. While automated simulation provides scalability, human interactions might reveal different behavior patterns and subtleties.
    *   The ethical strategy taxonomy might not be fully exhaustive, although the authors studied a good range of strategies.
    *   The work is limited to English language interactions, and how the models might perform across other cultures and languages is unaddressed.

The paper offers a valuable contribution by systematically studying the safety risks associated with LLM persuasion. The findings reveal critical gaps in existing safety alignment techniques and highlight the need for a more nuanced approach to ensuring ethical behavior in goal-driven conversational AI systems. The framework is valuable and well-designed and the findings significant.
